### Turing Test 2.0: The General Intelligence Threshold
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19550v1)
- **Authors**: Georgios Mappouras
- **Abstract**: With the rise of artificial intelligence (A.I.) and large language models like Chat-GPT, a new race for achieving artificial general intelligence (A.G.I) has started. While many speculate how and when A.I. will achieve A.G.I., there is no clear agreement on how A.G.I. can be detected in A.I. models, even when popular tools like the Turing test (and its modern variations) are used to measure their intelligence. In this work, we discuss why traditional methods like the Turing test do not suffice for measuring or detecting A.G.I. and provide a new, practical method that can be used to decide if a (computer or any other) system has reached or surpassed A.G.I. To achieve this, we make two new contributions. First, we present a clear definition for general intelligence (G.I.) and set a G.I. threshold (G.I.T.) that can be used to distinguish between systems that achieve A.G.I. and systems that do not. Second, we present a new framework on how to construct tests that can detect if a system has achieved G.I. in a simple, comprehensive, and clear-cut fail/pass way. We call this novel framework the Turing Tests 2.0. We then demonstrate real-life examples of applying tests that follow our Turing Tests 2.0 framework on modern A.I. models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of defining and detecting Artificial General Intelligence (AGI). It argues that traditional methods like the Turing Test are insufficient for measuring AGI and proposes a new framework called Turing Tests 2.0. The framework is based on the concept of a "General Intelligence Threshold" (GIT), which a system must cross to be considered AGI. The GIT is defined as the ability of a system to operate in a "generative state," where it can extract new useful information (U.I.) from previously non-useful information (N.U.I.).  The paper outlines specific criteria for designing tests that adhere to the Turing Test 2.0 framework, and it then provides examples of applying these tests to popular Large Language Models (LLMs). The paper concludes that current LLMs fail to meet the GIT, indicating that they have not achieved AGI. The authors propose more advanced tests, including Single Discipline and Generational tests. They also briefly discuss potential dangers of over-trusting A.I. systems without achieving G.I.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its re-framing of the AGI problem through the lens of information generation and the definition of the General Intelligence Threshold (GIT). Defining AGI as the ability to generate new *useful* information from previously non-useful data is a relatively new and thought-provoking perspective. The development of the Turing Test 2.0 framework provides a concrete methodology for evaluating AGI, making it a significant contribution beyond a purely theoretical discussion. However, the generative nature has been pointed out in research like the Lovelace test, the real contribution being the operationalization of such concept into a clear framework of testing.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of A.I. – how to define and measure AGI. The current lack of consensus on this issue hinders progress in developing and evaluating AGI systems. The Turing Test 2.0 framework offers a practical approach for designing and conducting tests that can help researchers and developers determine whether a system has achieved AGI. The application of these tests to LLMs is particularly relevant, given the recent advances in this area and the widespread debate about their capabilities. The discussion on advanced tests, such as Single Discipline and Generational tests, points toward future directions for AGI research. The dangers of over-trusting A.I. systems is also a very relevant observation as LLMs are integrated in critical infrastructures.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-defined framework:** The Turing Test 2.0 framework provides a clear and structured methodology for evaluating AGI.
    *   **Practical examples:** The paper demonstrates the applicability of the framework by applying it to popular LLMs.
    *   **Emphasis on information generation:** The focus on information generation as a key characteristic of AGI is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Addresses real-world concerns:** The paper discusses the potential dangers of over-trusting A.I. systems, highlighting the importance of addressing the AGI problem.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Subjectivity in "usefulness":** The notion of "useful information" is somewhat subjective and context-dependent. The paper could benefit from providing more guidance on how to determine whether information is "useful" in a given context. While it provides some criteria, practical operationalization may still be challenging.
    *   **Limitations of the chosen tests:** The paper is limited to only certain types of tests (image generation from text). The ability of LLMs to generate the hexagonal shapes from a standard stop sign might be something better acquired through balancing the training sets than demonstrating real A.I.. As such the limited range of experiments restricts generalizability.
    *   **Assumptions about LLM training data:** The arguments on what information can be expected on LLMs based on what information is present in the train dataset can be problematic as the same information can be learned through other means like reasoning, therefore these assumption can be flawed.
    *   **Simplification of information dynamics:**  While the high-level discussion of information dynamics is interesting, it's presented in a simplified manner.  The complexities of human learning and knowledge creation might not be fully captured by a simple U.I./N.U.I. distinction.
    *   The definition of G.I. and A.G.I., although is the premise of the paper, heavily depends on the definition of human intelligence, in a sense, being too close to the same problems of older definitions.
    *   Limited scope in A.I.: Given that the test is limited to LLMs, this means that several sectors of the industry, like automated robotics are left out, in a sense limiting the test as A.I can exist without natural language processing.
    *   Difficult to assess in a deterministic way: There is little information on how the LLMs should be assessed in case that are able to pass Turing Test 2.0. While "consistent ability to solve T" is one criteria, however, it remains vague, as well as how much effort must be given by human tester to teach/show to the A.I. how to solve task T.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Turing Test 2.0 framework has the potential to influence future research in AGI by providing a concrete methodology for evaluating systems. The framework could also be used to guide the development of AGI systems by focusing on the ability to generate new information. The emphasis on information generation may also lead to new approaches for training and developing A.I. models.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of AGI by re-framing the problem through the lens of information generation and proposing the Turing Test 2.0 framework. The application of the framework to LLMs is also relevant and timely. However, the subjectivity in the definition of "useful information," the limited scope of the tests (specifically image generation through LLMs), and the assumptions about A.I. models training data, detract from the paper's overall impact. Moreover, the current Turing Test 2.0 heavily depends on the old concepts used for human intelligence, and it lacks a determinist way of assessing LLMs. Given these limitations, I assign the paper a score of 6.5. The paper presents a promising approach for addressing the AGI problem, but further research is needed to address the weaknesses and fully realize the framework's potential.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Customising Electricity Contracts at Scale with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19551v1)
- **Authors**: Jochen L. Cremer
- **Abstract**: The electricity system becomes more complex, connecting massive numbers of end-users and distributed generators. Adding or removing grid connections requires expert studies to align technical constraints with user requests. In times of labour shortages, carrying out these studies represents a significant amount of time that engineers at system operators spend in planning departments. As time is limited, only standard block connectivity contracts can be offered to end-users, or the requests pile up. Even if offers are made, these often do not perfectly match the user's requirements, leading to overpaying or underusing the grid capacity. This paper investigates whether end-users can negotiate individual, flexible time-of-use contracts directly with the grid using Large Language Models (LLM) in chats at scale. The LLM-based chat has direct access to a model of the grid and studies the grid's technical constraints just as an expert engineer. The advantage of this system is that end-users can directly interact with grid models through natural language; no intermediate is needed to service, analyse, study, assess, advise, consult and engineer. This initial study paves the way toward developing this tailored LLM system, resulting in possible high-efficiency gains for grid planning and customer management.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) in a chat interface to customize electricity contracts at scale, particularly for end-users connecting to the grid. It addresses the growing complexity of the electricity system with distributed generation and the limitations of standardized contracts and manual expert studies. The authors propose an LLM-based chat system that directly interacts with grid models, simulates technical constraints, and enables end-users to negotiate flexible, time-of-use contracts. The system aims to reduce the need for expert intervention and unlock the potential for demand-side management and grid flexibility that are currently difficult to achieve due to labor shortages and the complexity of individual consultations. The paper presents initial use cases in low voltage (residential), medium voltage (SMEs) and high voltage (transmission) systems, demonstrating robustness to user input variations, connection request verification, and chat-enabled maintenance planning. It also examines privacy and security concerns. The paper uses IEEE test systems to prove the effectiveness of the model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of LLMs with grid models to automate the process of customizing electricity contracts. While LLMs have been used in various domains for customer interaction, their application to the complex domain of power system planning and contract negotiation, with direct interaction with grid simulation tools, is innovative. The approach attempts to bridge the gap between end-users with limited technical knowledge and the intricate constraints of the electricity grid. The focus on enabling more granular control and flexibility for end-users is also important.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of this work is substantial. If successful, the proposed system can:

    *   Increase efficiency in grid planning and customer management.
    *   Unlock demand-side management potential by making customized contracts accessible to a wider audience.
    *   Facilitate better integration of distributed energy resources (DERs) and renewable energy.
    *   Reduce reliance on scarce engineering expertise.
    *   Help industrial consumers access electricity in constrained MV grids.
    *   Improve outage scheduling by considering system constraints in near-real-time and presenting alternatives to users.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper identifies a well-defined and relevant problem: the limitations of current approaches to connecting and managing DERs in a complex electricity system.
    *   **Innovative Approach:** The integration of LLMs with grid models and simulation tools is a promising direction.
    *   **Use Cases:** The paper provides concrete use cases that demonstrate the potential applicability of the system. The IEEE test systems provide validation in a relatively standard format.
    *   **Addresses Key Challenges:** The paper acknowledges important challenges such as security, privacy, and robustness to user input variations.
    *   **Comprehensive Methodology:** Methodology is relatively clear through use of a functional paradigm with LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Experimental Validation:** The experimental results are somewhat preliminary. While the paper demonstrates the feasibility of the approach, it lacks extensive quantitative data to fully assess the system's performance, scalability, and cost-effectiveness in a real-world setting. It lacks a strong comparison with the other state-of-the-art.
    *   **Simplifications and Assumptions:** The paper makes certain simplifying assumptions in the use cases. The complexity of real-world grid operations and customer behavior may not be fully captured in the simulations.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** Scalability to large and diverse grids remains a major question, especially related to how one can manage large grid models. While this is briefly mentioned, there’s no detail on how scalability would be guaranteed.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Data Quality:** LLM integration with grid models requires high-quality grid data. The dependency of the system's performance on the accuracy and completeness of grid data is not addressed adequately.
    *   **Lack of Discussion on Governance and Oversight:** A clear framework for governance and human oversight of the automated contract negotiation process is needed, especially to ensure fairness and avoid potential biases in the LLM. This is just briefly touched with engineers overseeing.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in the area of smart grids, customer engagement, and the application of AI to power systems.

*   **Justification:** While the paper presents a novel and promising approach, the limited experimental validation and simplifying assumptions prevent it from receiving a higher score. It demonstrates the feasibility of the concept and raises important questions for future research.

**Score: 7**

The score reflects the paper's innovation and potential significance, balanced against its limitations in experimental validation and its simplifying assumptions. Future research should focus on addressing these limitations and conducting more rigorous testing in real-world scenarios.

- **Classification**: eess.SY
- **Score**: 7/10

### On scalable and efficient training of diffusion samplers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19552v1)
- **Authors**: Minkyu Kim, Kiyoung Seong, Dongyeop Woo, Sungsoo Ahn, Minsu Kim
- **Abstract**: We address the challenge of training diffusion models to sample from unnormalized energy distributions in the absence of data, the so-called diffusion samplers. Although these approaches have shown promise, they struggle to scale in more demanding scenarios where energy evaluations are expensive and the sampling space is high-dimensional. To address this limitation, we propose a scalable and sample-efficient framework that properly harmonizes the powerful classical sampling method and the diffusion sampler. Specifically, we utilize Monte Carlo Markov chain (MCMC) samplers with a novelty-based auxiliary energy as a Searcher to collect off-policy samples, using an auxiliary energy function to compensate for exploring modes the diffusion sampler rarely visits. These off-policy samples are then combined with on-policy data to train the diffusion sampler, thereby expanding its coverage of the energy landscape. Furthermore, we identify primacy bias, i.e., the preference of samplers for early experience during training, as the main cause of mode collapse during training, and introduce a periodic re-initialization trick to resolve this issue. Our method significantly improves sample efficiency on standard benchmarks for diffusion samplers and also excels at higher-dimensional problems and real-world molecular conformer generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Search-Guided Diffusion Samplers (SGDS), a framework designed to improve the scalability and sample efficiency of diffusion models when sampling from unnormalized energy distributions without readily available data. SGDS harmonizes classical MCMC methods with diffusion samplers. It employs an MCMC "Searcher" guided by a novelty-based auxiliary energy to discover under-explored modes.  These off-policy samples are then used in conjunction with on-policy data to train a diffusion "Learner" via a trajectory balance objective. The method also addresses "primacy bias" (the tendency to over-rely on early training data) by periodically re-initializing the Learner.  The authors demonstrate that SGDS improves sample efficiency on benchmark tasks, including Gaussian mixtures, the Manywell problem, particle simulations (LJ-13 and LJ-55), and real-world molecular conformer generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable degree of novelty by integrating several existing ideas in a non-trivial way. The core innovation lies in the synergy between the MCMC searcher and the diffusion learner. While off-policy training in diffusion samplers is not entirely new, the specific combination of an MCMC searcher with RND exploration rewards, trajectory balance learning, and periodic re-initialization to combat primacy bias is a distinctive contribution. The identification and mitigation of primacy bias within the context of diffusion sampler training are also noteworthy.
* **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical challenge addressed: training diffusion samplers when energy evaluations are expensive and the sampling space is high-dimensional. Current diffusion samplers struggle in these scenarios. SGDS demonstrates improved sample efficiency and scalability, potentially making diffusion models more viable for complex, real-world problems, particularly in scientific applications (e.g., molecular dynamics). The demonstration on Alanine Dipeptide adds credibility to this claim.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Key Limitation:** The paper directly tackles a known weakness of diffusion samplers.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The method is evaluated across multiple benchmark tasks, demonstrating generalizability.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  Ablation studies are included to validate the contributions of individual components.
    *   **Real-World Application:** The application to molecular conformer generation showcases the practical relevance of the method.
    *   **Clear Explanation of Primacy Bias:** The identification and discussion of primacy bias provides a valuable insight.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Component Dependence:** SGDS relies on the efficacy of both the MCMC searcher and the RND-driven exploration. If either component performs poorly, the overall performance could be limited. While the authors address this by using established MCMC and RND methods, the dependence is still present.
    * **Tuning Requirements:**  The performance is sensitive to the parameter *alpha* in the RND reward. The paper lacks a detailed analysis of how to choose the right value.  While mentioned, more details on the automated strategy to avoid bias are needed. The tuning of this parameter diminishes the practical benefit.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** The improvements, although present, are not groundbreaking. Several baselines, when properly tuned, can achieve comparable performance on some of the tasks.  The value is in the combination and improved efficiency.
    * **Clarity:** The paper could benefit from even clearer explantations and visualizations of complex dynamics in the models.

* **Potential Impact:** If the method can be further refined and generalized to reduce sensitivity to parameter tuning, it has the potential to become a standard approach for training diffusion samplers in challenging scenarios. The focus on scalability and sample efficiency is highly relevant for many applications.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7/10**.

The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing a practical limitation of diffusion samplers and proposing a coherent framework. The integration of MCMC, trajectory balance, and RND demonstrates a good understanding of the field. The empirical results validate the effectiveness of the approach, especially in terms of scalability and sample efficiency.  However, some concerns weaken its impact. The dependence on well-tuned MCMC and RND, as well as the somewhat incremental performance gains compared to heavily tuned baselines, limit its transformational potential.  Moreover, the clarity could be enhanced in several areas.  However, the paper provides a valuable step toward more efficient training of diffusion samplers, and it offers a useful analysis of primacy bias within this context.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### Aggregated Structural Representation with Large Language Models for Human-Centric Layout Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19554v1)
- **Authors**: Jiongchao Jin, Shengchu Zhao, Dajun Chen, Wei Jiang, Yong Li
- **Abstract**: Time consumption and the complexity of manual layout design make automated layout generation a critical task, especially for multiple applications across different mobile devices. Existing graph-based layout generation approaches suffer from limited generative capability, often resulting in unreasonable and incompatible outputs. Meanwhile, vision based generative models tend to overlook the original structural information, leading to component intersections and overlaps. To address these challenges, we propose an Aggregation Structural Representation (ASR) module that integrates graph networks with large language models (LLMs) to preserve structural information while enhancing generative capability. This novel pipeline utilizes graph features as hierarchical prior knowledge, replacing the traditional Vision Transformer (ViT) module in multimodal large language models (MLLM) to predict full layout information for the first time. Moreover, the intermediate graph matrix used as input for the LLM is human editable, enabling progressive, human centric design generation. A comprehensive evaluation on the RICO dataset demonstrates the strong performance of ASR, both quantitatively using mean Intersection over Union (mIoU), and qualitatively through a crowdsourced user study. Additionally, sampling on relational features ensures diverse layout generation, further enhancing the adaptability and creativity of the proposed approach.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary, critical evaluation, and novelty/significance score for the provided paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Aggregated Structural Representation (ASR), a novel approach for automated UI layout generation that combines graph neural networks (GNNs) and large language models (LLMs). ASR extracts hierarchical representations from UI layouts, leveraging both semantic and positional relationships between UI elements. It uses a GNN to encode these representations and a decoder to supervise component relationships, guiding the LLM to generate accurate and diverse layouts. A key feature is the human-editable relation matrix, enabling user interaction and customization during the design process. The approach is evaluated on the RICO dataset, demonstrating superior performance in terms of mIoU, relation error, overlap, and FID score compared to existing methods. A user study further validates the ASR approach, highlighting the adaptability and human-centric design capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation (Novelty and Significance):**

The paper presents an interesting fusion of graph-based representation learning with the generative power of LLMs for UI layout generation, however, the novelty is somewhat incremental.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Human-Centric Design:** The editable relation matrix and the ability to incorporate human input into the layout generation process is a valuable contribution. This allows for personalized and iterative design, which is often lacking in fully automated approaches. This enhances the usability of the model for practical applications.
    *   **Structural Preservation:** The ASR module's focus on preserving structural information from the original layout addresses a key limitation of many vision-based generative models. This leads to more reasonable and structurally sound layouts, reducing component intersections and overlaps. The usage of GNN's to encode this information is appropriate.
    *   **Strong Quantitative Results:** The paper demonstrates strong quantitative performance on the RICO dataset, surpassing existing methods in terms of mIoU, relation error, overlap, and FID score. This provides compelling evidence of the effectiveness of the ASR approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation**: Incorporating a user study along with the other qualitative and quantitative analysis helps provide a thorough validation of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of GNNs and LLMs is effective, it's not entirely novel. The core idea of using GNNs for structural information and LLMs for generation has been explored in other contexts. The novelty lies in the specific architecture and application to UI layout generation, making the contribution incrementally innovative.
    *   **Dataset Dependence:** The RICO dataset is a common benchmark in UI layout generation, and the paper demonstrates good results on it. However, the ASR's performance and generalization capability on other, more diverse datasets, or datasets with varied UI design styles, is not explored in sufficient depth.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture, involving GNN encoders/decoders and MLLMs, is inherently complex. The ablation studies help to justify the components but don't fully address the computational and memory demands, limiting potential deployability.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Failure Cases:** While the paper presents successful layout generations, it lacks a thorough discussion of potential failure cases. Understanding the limitations of the ASR module, and the types of UI structures it struggles to generate, would provide a more complete assessment of its capabilities.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing field of automated UI layout generation, with potential applications in mobile app development, web design, and accessibility. The human-centric design capabilities of ASR make it a valuable tool for UI designers, allowing for efficient and customizable layout creation. However, the relatively incremental novelty and limitations in the scope of evaluation prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid technical contribution with well-demonstrated improvements in UI layout generation. The human-centric design aspect and the emphasis on structural preservation are valuable. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and there's room for improvement in terms of dataset diversity, addressing complexity concerns, and providing a more thorough discussion of failure cases. While the quantitative results are compelling, the paper lacks a substantial theoretical contribution or a paradigm shift in the field. The impact is likely to be confined to incremental improvements in UI layout generation tools and workflows, rather than sparking significant new research directions.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### EuroCon: Benchmarking Parliament Deliberation for Political Consensus Finding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19558v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaowei Zhang, Minghua Yi, Mengmeng Wang, Fengshuo Bai, Zilong Zheng, Yipeng Kang, Yaodong Yang
- **Abstract**: Achieving political consensus is crucial yet challenging for the effective functioning of social governance. However, although frontier AI systems represented by large language models (LLMs) have developed rapidly in recent years, their capabilities on this scope are still understudied. In this paper, we introduce EuroCon, a novel benchmark constructed from 2,225 high-quality deliberation records of the European Parliament over 13 years, ranging from 2009 to 2022, to evaluate the ability of LLMs to reach political consensus among divergent party positions across diverse parliament settings. Specifically, EuroCon incorporates four factors to build each simulated parliament setting: specific political issues, political goals, participating parties, and power structures based on seat distribution. We also develop an evaluation framework for EuroCon to simulate real voting outcomes in different parliament settings, assessing whether LLM-generated resolutions meet predefined political goals. Our experimental results demonstrate that even state-of-the-art models remain undersatisfied with complex tasks like passing resolutions by a two-thirds majority and addressing security issues, while revealing some common strategies LLMs use to find consensus under different power structures, such as prioritizing the stance of the dominant party, highlighting EuroCon's promise as an effective platform for studying LLMs' ability to find political consensus.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "EuroCon: Benchmarking Parliament Deliberation for Political Consensus Finding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EuroCon, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to achieve political consensus in simulated parliamentary settings. The benchmark is constructed from 2,225 real deliberation records of the European Parliament (2009-2022). EuroCon incorporates four adjustable factors: political issues, political goals, participating parties, and power structures (seat distribution). The authors also develop an evaluation framework based on GPT-4o mini to simulate real voting outcomes and assess whether LLM-generated resolutions meet predefined political goals. Experiments using six LLMs demonstrate that even state-of-the-art models struggle with complex tasks like passing resolutions with a two-thirds majority and addressing security issues. The analysis also reveals common consensus-finding strategies used by LLMs, such as prioritizing the stance of the dominant party.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its creation of a **realistic and complex** benchmark specifically for political consensus finding. While prior work has explored LLMs in democratic deliberation and political analysis, EuroCon distinguishes itself by simulating real-world parliamentary settings with multiple interacting factors (issue, goals, parties, power). The open-ended evaluation framework using GPT-4o mini to simulate voting based on party stances is also a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant gap in understanding the capabilities of LLMs in navigating complex political scenarios. Political consensus-building is crucial for effective social governance, and the EuroCon benchmark provides a valuable tool for studying how LLMs can contribute (or fail to contribute) to this process. The identified challenges for current LLMs (e.g., two-thirds majority votes, security issues, strategic manipulation) highlight areas for future research and development.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Realistic Benchmark:** The use of real European Parliament data enhances the benchmark's realism and relevance.
    *   **Adjustable Factors:** The four adjustable factors allow for the creation of diverse and complex parliamentary scenarios.
    *   **Open-ended Evaluation:** The voting simulation framework based on GPT-4o mini provides a flexible and automated way to assess LLM-generated resolutions.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation of several LLMs, revealing their strengths and weaknesses in different parliamentary settings.
    *   **Analysis of LLM Strategies:** The identification of common consensus-finding strategies used by LLMs offers insights into their decision-making processes.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Data Processing Bias:** The use of LLMs in the data cleaning and stance summarization stages could introduce bias. The authors acknowledge this limitation, but the potential impact on the benchmark's objectivity should be considered.
    *   **Simplified Party Representation:** The paper treats political parties as monolithic entities, ignoring internal divisions and factions. This simplification may limit the benchmark's realism.
    *   **Simulated Voting Limitations:** While the GPT-4o mini-based voting simulation is a valuable approach, the reliance on LLM-based simulation introduces potential limitations compared to true human voting patterns. The simulation could simplify the complex dynamics of voting patterns and strategic interactions between parties. The decision to use GPT-40 mini could be also seen as a weakness. It sacrifices performance in certain dimensions in comparison to larger LLMs.
    *   **Limited Veto Scenarios:** The veto mechanism is oversimplified compared to the complex real-world dynamics.
*   **Potential Influence:** EuroCon has the potential to significantly influence research on LLMs and their application to political deliberation and consensus-building. It provides a standardized benchmark for comparing different LLMs and evaluating new techniques. The insights gained from EuroCon could inform the development of more sophisticated AI systems for supporting democratic processes. The dataset created here has the possibility of unlocking other research in political and social computing.
*   **Justification for Score:** While the benchmark has limitations, its novelty, significance, and the comprehensive evaluation performed justify a high score. The weaknesses highlight areas for future improvement, but they do not diminish the value of EuroCon as a valuable research resource.

Score: 8.5

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 8/10

### AMQA: An Adversarial Dataset for Benchmarking Bias of LLMs in Medicine and Healthcare
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19562v1)
- **Authors**: Ying Xiao, Jie Huang, Ruijuan He, Jing Xiao, Mohammad Reza Mousavi, Yepang Liu, Kezhi Li, Zhenpeng Chen, Jie M. Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are reaching expert-level accuracy on medical diagnosis questions, yet their mistakes and the biases behind them pose life-critical risks. Bias linked to race, sex, and socioeconomic status is already well known, but a consistent and automatic testbed for measuring it is missing. To fill this gap, this paper presents AMQA -- an Adversarial Medical Question-Answering dataset -- built for automated, large-scale bias evaluation of LLMs in medical QA. AMQA includes 4,806 medical QA pairs sourced from the United States Medical Licensing Examination (USMLE) dataset, generated using a multi-agent framework to create diverse adversarial descriptions and question pairs. Using AMQA, we benchmark five representative LLMs and find surprisingly substantial disparities: even GPT-4.1, the least biased model tested, answers privileged-group questions over 10 percentage points more accurately than unprivileged ones. Compared with the existing benchmark CPV, AMQA reveals 15% larger accuracy gaps on average between privileged and unprivileged groups. Our dataset and code are publicly available at https://github.com/XY-Showing/AMQA to support reproducible research and advance trustworthy, bias-aware medical AI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces AMQA, a new adversarial dataset designed to benchmark and evaluate biases in Large Language Models (LLMs) within medical question-answering scenarios.  AMQA contains 4,806 medical question-answer pairs derived from USMLE questions and crafted using a multi-agent framework to generate adversarial descriptions that vary demographic attributes (race, sex, socioeconomic status).  The key idea is to create counterfactual scenarios that isolate the impact of these attributes on LLM performance, revealing biases. The authors benchmarked several prominent LLMs using AMQA and discovered significant disparities in accuracy between privileged and unprivileged demographic groups. The paper emphasizes the need for automated and reproducible bias evaluation in medical AI, especially given the potential for life-critical consequences. They highlight that AMQA reveals larger accuracy gaps compared to existing benchmarks like CPV.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in the creation of the AMQA dataset and its associated multi-agent adversarial generation framework. While previous work has explored bias in medical LLMs, AMQA offers a specifically designed, controlled dataset to systematically assess and quantify these biases. The explicit focus on medical QA and the use of the USMLE as a source is also a strong point. The multi-agent approach to generating adversarial descriptions is a valuable contribution, going beyond simple attribute swapping to more nuanced contextual modifications. The comparison against CPV and demonstration of larger revealed accuracy gaps are compelling.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the paper stems from the growing importance of LLMs in healthcare and the critical need to ensure fair and unbiased application.  The paper directly addresses the problem of biased outcomes, which can have severe implications for patient care and health equity. The AMQA dataset provides a valuable resource for researchers to develop and test debiasing techniques, promoting more trustworthy and equitable medical AI systems. The benchmarking results demonstrate that even the most advanced LLMs exhibit considerable bias, highlighting the urgency of addressing this issue.  The publicly released dataset and code foster reproducible research in this crucial area. The detailed discussion of fairness metrics and statistical significance testing reinforces the rigor of their evaluation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and clear goals:** The paper clearly identifies a critical need and proposes a concrete solution.
    *   **Novel Dataset and Methodology:** The AMQA dataset and its generation framework are substantial contributions.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The benchmarking experiments use appropriate metrics and statistical analysis to demonstrate the presence of bias.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Public availability of the dataset and code promote further research.
    * **Careful manual curation:** The manual review process is extremely important and adds to the quality of this work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Binary Attributes:** The dataset focuses on binary sensitive attributes (Black/White, Male/Female, High/Low Income). This simplifies the analysis but might miss more nuanced biases related to intersectionality or other demographic groups (e.g. other race/ethnicities, other SES factors, gender identities, etc).  The authors acknowledge this in the Limitations section.
    *   **Scope of QA:** While the focus on USMLE-style questions is valuable, the generalizability of the findings to other medical contexts (e.g., clinical notes, doctor-patient interactions) may be limited.
    * **Reliance on Known Bias:** As acknowledged in the limitations section, the method relies on prior knowledge regarding which sensitive attributes might induce bias.

*   **Potential Influence:** AMQA is likely to become a valuable benchmark in the medical AI community, influencing future research on fairness auditing, bias mitigation, and responsible LLM deployment. The clear methodology and detailed analysis provide a solid foundation for follow-up studies.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to a vital and growing area of research.  The creation of AMQA fills a gap in existing benchmarks and provides a more controlled and effective means of evaluating bias in medical LLMs. While the limitations regarding binary attributes and the scope of QA are valid, the strengths in terms of novelty, rigor, and reproducibility outweigh these shortcomings. The significance of addressing bias in medical AI justifies a high score.

### LLM-Agent-Controller: A Universal Multi-Agent Large Language Model System as a Control Engineer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19567v1)
- **Authors**: Rasoul Zahedifar, Sayyed Ali Mirghasemi, Mahdieh Soleymani Baghshah, Alireza Taheri
- **Abstract**: This study presents the LLM-Agent-Controller, a multi-agent large language model (LLM) system developed to address a wide range of problems in control engineering (Control Theory). The system integrates a central controller agent with multiple specialized auxiliary agents, responsible for tasks such as controller design, model representation, control analysis, time-domain response, and simulation. A supervisor oversees high-level decision-making and workflow coordination, enhancing the system's reliability and efficiency. The LLM-Agent-Controller incorporates advanced capabilities, including Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), Chain-of-Thought reasoning, self-criticism and correction, efficient memory handling, and user-friendly natural language communication. It is designed to function without requiring users to have prior knowledge of Control Theory, enabling them to input problems in plain language and receive complete, real-time solutions. To evaluate the system, we propose new performance metrics assessing both individual agents and the system as a whole. We test five categories of Control Theory problems and benchmark performance across three advanced LLMs. Additionally, we conduct a comprehensive qualitative conversational analysis covering all key services. Results show that the LLM-Agent-Controller successfully solved 83% of general tasks, with individual agents achieving an average success rate of 87%. Performance improved with more advanced LLMs. This research demonstrates the potential of multi-agent LLM architectures to solve complex, domain-specific problems. By integrating specialized agents, supervisory control, and advanced reasoning, the LLM-Agent-Controller offers a scalable, robust, and accessible solution framework that can be extended to various technical domains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the LLM-Agent-Controller, a multi-agent system designed for control engineering problems. It uses a central controller agent coordinated by a supervisor, along with specialized agents for tasks like controller design, model representation, analysis, and simulation. The system leverages RAG, Chain-of-Thought reasoning, and memory management.  The authors propose new performance metrics to evaluate both individual agents and the system as a whole. The system is tested on various control theory problems using different LLMs. Results indicate successful task completion and improved performance with more advanced LLMs. The paper claims the architecture offers a scalable and accessible solution for control engineering tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

While multi-agent systems and LLMs have been applied to various domains, including some engineering tasks, the specific application to control engineering with the proposed architecture and level of integration appears relatively novel. The introduction of dedicated auxiliary agents with specialized roles for control engineering, supervised by a central LLM controller, distinguishes this work from simpler approaches. The proposition of dedicated metrics is also a specific, targeted addition. However, the core components (LLMs, multi-agent systems, RAG) are individually well-established. The novelty lies in their combination and application to this specific area.

**Significance:**

*   **Potential Impact:** The system has the potential to make control engineering tools more accessible to users without specialized knowledge, streamlining design and analysis processes.
*   **Technical Strengths:** The inclusion of multiple specialized agents enhances the system's adaptability and efficiency. The use of RAG and self-correction capabilities improves accuracy. The claimed versatility to different LLMs offers some practical value.
*   **Weaknesses and Limitations:**
    *   **Overstated Claims:** The claim of a "universal" solution framework is likely an overreach. Control engineering is a broad field, and the paper focuses primarily on core, possibly simplified tasks.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While performance is benchmarked across categories, the complexity of problems addressed isn't deeply explored.
    *   **Missing Comparisons:** A strong evaluation would include comparison against traditional control design methods or existing control-specific software tools. This is noticeably lacking.
    *   **Lack of Depth in Real-World Applications:** The paper discusses potential applications, but there's no significant exploration of how the system performs in scenarios with real-world complexities (noise, uncertainties, non-linearities, etc.). The operational cost reported seems very low and might be misleading in practical scenarios.
    *   **Limited Detail on Tool Implementation:** While there's a claim of over 140 tools, the description of these tools is superficial. The precise nature and complexity of these functions would need clarification.
    *   **Metric Validity:** While the proposed metrics are logical, their practical value and robustness need to be critically examined by others in the field.
    *   **Subjectivity in Evaluation:** The evaluation relies on success/failure criteria which might involve some degree of subjectivity, especially with complex systems.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a system that demonstrates a functional integration of LLMs for control engineering. However, the limitations related to the scope of evaluation, comparison to existing methods, and validation in real-world scenarios significantly temper the claims of a "universal" solution. The lack of comparison to traditional methods or custom engineering packages is a serious omission. More work is needed to establish the system's practical utility. The paper's impact on the field depends on its ability to demonstrably solve complex, real-world control problems better than existing methods.

### MSD-LLM: Predicting Ship Detention in Port State Control Inspections with Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19568v1)
- **Authors**: Jiongchao Jin, Xiuju Fu, Xiaowei Gao, Tao Cheng, Ran Yan
- **Abstract**: Maritime transportation is the backbone of global trade, making ship inspection essential for ensuring maritime safety and environmental protection. Port State Control (PSC), conducted by national ports, enforces compliance with safety regulations, with ship detention being the most severe consequence, impacting both ship schedules and company reputations. Traditional machine learning methods for ship detention prediction are limited by the capacity of representation learning and thus suffer from low accuracy. Meanwhile, autoencoder-based deep learning approaches face challenges due to the severe data imbalance in learning historical PSC detention records. To address these limitations, we propose Maritime Ship Detention with Large Language Models (MSD-LLM), integrating a dual robust subspace recovery (DSR) layer-based autoencoder with a progressive learning pipeline to handle imbalanced data and extract meaningful PSC representations. Then, a large language model groups and ranks features to identify likely detention cases, enabling dynamic thresholding for flexible detention predictions. Extensive evaluations on 31,707 PSC inspection records from the Asia-Pacific region show that MSD-LLM outperforms state-of-the-art methods more than 12\% on Area Under the Curve (AUC) for Singapore ports. Additionally, it demonstrates robustness to real-world challenges, making it adaptable to diverse maritime risk assessment scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MSD-LLM: Predicting Ship Detention in Port State Control Inspections with Large Language Model" introduces a novel approach to predicting ship detentions during Port State Control (PSC) inspections.  The method, MSD-LLM, combines a dual robust subspace recovery (DSR) layer-based autoencoder with a large language model (LLM). The autoencoder learns robust representations from historical PSC data, addressing the challenge of severe data imbalance (detained ships are rare).  The LLM then groups and ranks features extracted by the autoencoder to predict detention probabilities, providing a continuous confidence score for each ship.  The authors demonstrate that MSD-LLM outperforms traditional machine learning and deep learning methods on a dataset of PSC inspection records from the Asia-Pacific region, especially for Singapore ports. It also showed the robustness for practical real-world scenario.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach.  The combination of a DSR-based autoencoder with an LLM for ship detention prediction is a relatively new concept. It addresses a specific, well-defined problem within maritime logistics and risk management. The application of LLMs for this task has been limited, making this a worthwhile exploration. The progressive learning strategy and the grouping/ranking strategy within the LLM further contributes to its uniqueness.

*   **Significance:** Ship detention prediction is an important problem with economic and safety implications.  Improved prediction accuracy could lead to more efficient PSC inspections, enhanced maritime safety, and reduced operational disruptions for shipping companies.  The demonstrated performance improvements over existing methods are significant, suggesting that MSD-LLM has the potential to be a valuable tool for port authorities and other stakeholders.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Focus:**  The paper tackles a practical and relevant problem in maritime transportation.
    *   **Methodological Innovation:** The integration of DSR-based autoencoders with LLMs represents a significant methodological step. The way the LLM is used for ranking and not simple binary classification adds value. The progressive learning is very clever to handle imbalanced data.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results, comparing MSD-LLM against several state-of-the-art baselines on a substantial real-world dataset. The ablation studies are important in dissecting the contribution of each module.
    *   **Robustness Analysis:** The evaluation includes testing on both global and Singapore-specific port data, demonstrating robustness to variations in data distributions.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and its advantages.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Specificity:** While the dataset is significant, it's limited to the Tokyo MoU region.  The generalizability of MSD-LLM to other regions with different PSC practices and data characteristics is not thoroughly explored.
    *   **LLM Complexity:** The choice of the LLM Qwen-VL-2.5 could be questioned if simpler (and more readily available) LLMs could yield similar performance. It might be argued that the DSR layer already captures most of the information, and the LLM serves as a "nice-to-have" ranking function. A comparison with a simpler LLM could strengthen the argument for the more complex one.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Training LLMs is computationally expensive. The paper mentions using multiple A100 GPUs, making the method potentially inaccessible to smaller port authorities with limited resources. A discussion of the computational cost vs. performance trade-offs would be valuable.
    *   **Interpretability:** While LLMs can provide confidence scores, the interpretability of the detention predictions remains limited. Understanding *why* a particular ship is flagged for detention is crucial for effective PSC. More work is needed to explore the explainability of MSD-LLM's decisions.
    * The impact of prompt engineering is not clearly explored. More description could be added.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in maritime AI, particularly in ship detention prediction and other port risk assessment applications. The framework of combining representation learning with LLMs could be adapted to other domains with similar challenges.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to a significant problem. The empirical results are strong, demonstrating clear improvements over existing methods. However, the limitations related to dataset specificity, LLM complexity, interpretability, and computational cost slightly temper the overall score. Further work to address these weaknesses could significantly enhance the impact of the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### DocMEdit: Towards Document-Level Model Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19572v1)
- **Authors**: Li Zeng, Zeming Liu, Chong Feng, Heyan Huang, Yuhang Guo
- **Abstract**: Model editing aims to correct errors and outdated knowledge in the Large language models (LLMs) with minimal cost. Prior research has proposed a variety of datasets to assess the effectiveness of these model editing methods. However, most existing datasets only require models to output short phrases or sentences, overlooks the widespread existence of document-level tasks in the real world, raising doubts about their practical usability. Aimed at addressing this limitation and promoting the application of model editing in real-world scenarios, we propose the task of document-level model editing. To tackle such challenges and enhance model capabilities in practical settings, we introduce \benchmarkname, a dataset focused on document-level model editing, characterized by document-level inputs and outputs, extrapolative, and multiple facts within a single edit. We propose a series of evaluation metrics and experiments. The results show that the difficulties in document-level model editing pose challenges for existing model editing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and rigorous critical evaluation of the DocMEdit paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new task called document-level model editing, which aims to correct errors and outdated knowledge in large language models (LLMs) at the document level. To support research in this area, the authors present DocMEdit, a novel benchmark dataset containing document-level inputs and outputs with multiple facts requiring editing. The paper also proposes a series of evaluation metrics tailored for document-level model editing and conducts experiments to assess the performance of existing model editing methods on DocMEdit. The results demonstrate that current methods struggle with this task, exhibiting low accuracy and significant side effects. The authors further analyze the challenges associated with document-level editing, identifying factors such as document length, fact length, the number of facts, and fact updates that impact the effectiveness of editing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its introduction of the document-level model editing task and the associated DocMEdit dataset. Existing model editing benchmarks primarily focus on short phrases or sentences, whereas DocMEdit addresses the more complex and practical scenario of editing entire documents. This is a valuable contribution as it shifts the focus towards real-world applications of model editing. The dataset creation methodology leveraging Wikipedia updates is sound.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its recognition of the limitations of current model editing approaches in handling document-level tasks. By highlighting these limitations and providing a benchmark for future research, the paper encourages the development of more robust and practical model editing techniques. The paper effectively demonstrates the challenges of extrapolative editing, handling longer contexts, and managing multiple facts within a single document, which are common in real-world scenarios. The paper also identifies the limitations of RAG based methods which are not designed for edits relating to the facts in a document.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly defines the document-level model editing task and its challenges.
    *   High-quality dataset: The DocMEdit dataset is well-constructed, documented, and publicly available. The scale and detail presented are excellent.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation: The paper proposes appropriate evaluation metrics and conducts thorough experiments.
    *   Insightful analysis: The analysis of the factors affecting model editing performance provides valuable insights for future research.
    *   Error analysis: The detailed error analysis provides avenues for potential solutions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited exploration of novel solutions: The paper primarily focuses on evaluating existing methods and does not propose any novel model editing techniques specifically designed for document-level tasks. The work remains largely diagnostic, rather than prescriptive.
    *   Dependency on Wikipedia: The dataset construction relies heavily on Wikipedia updates, which may introduce biases or limitations.
    *   The authors indicate limitations with compute as well and therefore perhaps may not be using optimal models.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper is likely to influence future research in model editing by shifting the focus towards document-level tasks.
    *   The DocMEdit dataset will serve as a valuable resource for evaluating and comparing different model editing methods.
    *   The identified challenges and insights will guide the development of more effective and practical model editing techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of model editing by identifying a significant gap in existing benchmarks and providing a new resource for addressing this gap. While it does not propose novel solutions, the comprehensive evaluation and analysis of existing methods provide valuable insights and directions for future research. The DocMEdit dataset is a significant asset to the community. The identified limitations also suggest areas for future work, such as exploring novel model editing techniques specifically designed for document-level tasks and addressing the biases introduced by relying on Wikipedia updates.
