### Large Language Models in Code Co-generation for Safe Autonomous Vehicles
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19658v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Nouri, Beatriz Cabrero-Daniel, Zhennan Fei, Krishna Ronanki, Håkan Sivencrona, Christian Berger
- **Abstract**: Software engineers in various industrial domains are already using Large Language Models (LLMs) to accelerate the process of implementing parts of software systems. When considering its potential use for ADAS or AD systems in the automotive context, there is a need to systematically assess this new setup: LLMs entail a well-documented set of risks for safety-related systems' development due to their stochastic nature. To reduce the effort for code reviewers to evaluate LLM-generated code, we propose an evaluation pipeline to conduct sanity-checks on the generated code. We compare the performance of six state-of-the-art LLMs (CodeLlama, CodeGemma, DeepSeek-r1, DeepSeek-Coders, Mistral, and GPT-4) on four safety-related programming tasks. Additionally, we qualitatively analyse the most frequent faults generated by these LLMs, creating a failure-mode catalogue to support human reviewers. Finally, the limitations and capabilities of LLMs in code generation, and the use of the proposed pipeline in the existing process, are discussed.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for code co-generation in the context of safe autonomous vehicles. It proposes a "Generate fast, Eliminate fast" approach by integrating rapid checks using a simulation environment (esmini) to evaluate LLM-generated code before human review.  The authors compare the performance of six state-of-the-art LLMs (CodeLlama, CodeGemma, DeepSeek-r1, DeepSeek-Coders, Mistral, and GPT-4) on four safety-related programming tasks and qualitatively analyze the most frequent faults generated. The aim is to assess the viability, limitations, and capabilities of LLMs and to propose necessary adaptations to existing software engineering processes for safe integration of LLM-generated code. The paper emphasizes the importance of avoiding benchmark leakage and creating new evaluation methods to ensure LLMs meet expectations in real-world use-cases. The results highlight the varying performance of different LLMs across tasks with GPT-4 showing the most promising, but still limited, results in complex tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a highly relevant and important problem: the safe integration of LLMs into the development of safety-critical systems like autonomous vehicles.
*   **Practical Approach:** The proposed "Generate fast, Eliminate fast" approach, utilizing a simulation environment for rapid sanity checks, is practical and addresses a key challenge in using LLMs for code generation: the need to quickly identify and discard unusable code.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper conducts a reasonably comprehensive evaluation of several LLMs on different tasks with both quantitative and qualitative analysis.
*   **Focus on Safety:** The focus on safety-related programming tasks and the analysis of potential failure modes is crucial for this application domain. The identification of potential risks for safety-critical automotive functions like selecting wrong thresholds and failing to consider edge cases is valuable.
*   **Addressing Benchmark Leakage:** The paper acknowledges and attempts to mitigate the issue of benchmark leakage by designing novel tests, which is important for getting a true understanding of the LLMs capabilities.
*   **Systematic Methodology:** The paper provides a clear and systematic methodology which allows for replication by other research groups.
*   **Adaptation to Existing Processes:** The suggestion of how to integrate LLM pipelines into existing software engineering processes is a step forward to see how this can translate in practice.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to a specific set of tasks and a specific simulation environment (esmini). While the tasks are relevant, the generalizability of the findings to other types of autonomous driving functions or other simulation environments might be limited.
*   **Open-Source vs. Closed Source:** The direct comparison of open source vs. a closed source model (GPT-4) can be misleading given the resources available to train these different models.
*   **Lack of deeper integration insights:** The paper stops short on providing clear and detailed process adaptation suggestions for LLM integration, remaining somewhat generic on the recommended V&V activities.
*   **LLM selection justification**: The paper mentions that the LLMs were selected based on popularity but a clear rationale is missing for how they were chosen specifically and why others where not considered.
*   **Limited discussion of trade-offs**: The paper briefly mentions trade-offs between complexity and LLM selection. However, further elaboration is needed on how to properly navigate these trade-offs in a real-world development setting.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution by exploring the application of LLMs in the context of safety-critical systems and proposing a practical approach for integrating them into existing software development workflows.  The focus on safety and the effort to avoid benchmark leakage are significant. The findings regarding the performance of different LLMs on safety-related tasks, especially the limitations observed, are important for researchers and practitioners in the field.  However, the limited scope of the evaluation and the lack of more detailed integration guidelines slightly limit the overall novelty and potential impact.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution that takes on an important problem. It does this by proposing a working methodology and provides initial findings. It balances between practical utility and scientific rigour. While the paper's scope of experiments and analysis are relatively limited, the paper highlights a practical problem for using LLMs in automated driving.

### GenKI: Enhancing Open-Domain Question Answering with Knowledge Integration and Controllable Generation in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19660v1)
- **Authors**: Tingjia Shen, Hao Wang, Chuan Qin, Ruijun Sun, Yang Song, Defu Lian, Hengshu Zhu, Enhong Chen
- **Abstract**: Open-domain question answering (OpenQA) represents a cornerstone in natural language processing (NLP), primarily focused on extracting answers from unstructured textual data. With the rapid advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), LLM-based OpenQA methods have reaped the benefits of emergent understanding and answering capabilities enabled by massive parameters compared to traditional methods. However, most of these methods encounter two critical challenges: how to integrate knowledge into LLMs effectively and how to adaptively generate results with specific answer formats for various task situations. To address these challenges, we propose a novel framework named GenKI, which aims to improve the OpenQA performance by exploring Knowledge Integration and controllable Generation on LLMs simultaneously. Specifically, we first train a dense passage retrieval model to retrieve associated knowledge from a given knowledge base. Subsequently, we introduce a novel knowledge integration model that incorporates the retrieval knowledge into instructions during fine-tuning to intensify the model. Furthermore, to enable controllable generation in LLMs, we leverage a certain fine-tuned LLM and an ensemble based on text consistency incorporating all coherence, fluency, and answer format assurance. Finally, extensive experiments conducted on the TriviaQA, MSMARCO, and CMRC2018 datasets, featuring diverse answer formats, have demonstrated the effectiveness of GenKI with comparison of state-of-the-art baselines. Moreover, ablation studies have disclosed a linear relationship between the frequency of retrieved knowledge and the model's ability to recall knowledge accurately against the ground truth. Our code of GenKI is available at https://github.com/USTC-StarTeam/GenKI
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "GenKI: Enhancing Open-Domain Question Answering with Knowledge Integration and Controllable Generation in Large Language Models" introduces a novel framework (GenKI) to improve OpenQA performance.  GenKI tackles two key challenges in using LLMs for OpenQA: 1) effectively integrating external knowledge and 2) controlling the answer format. The framework consists of three stages: knowledge retrieval, knowledge integration (using a loss combining autoregressive training and supervised fine-tuning to directly incorporate retrieved knowledge into the LLM's parameters), and controllable generation (using a fine-tuned LLM and an ensemble method based on text consistency to ensure coherence, fluency, and answer format compliance). The paper presents experimental results on TriviaQA, MSMARCO, and CMRC2018 datasets, showing GenKI's effectiveness compared to existing methods. Ablation studies are also conducted to analyze the impact of various components and knowledge integration aspects.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents incremental but important novelty.
    *   The three-stage paradigm (retrieval, integration, generation) is well motivated by limitations of the conventional RAG framework which treats the LLM as only an instruction interpreter, discounting its knowledge storage capacity.
    *   The Knowledge Integration module's method of incorporating retrieved knowledge through a combined autoregressive and supervised fine-tuning loss is a notable departure from prompt-based methods. Storing domain knowledge in parameters instead of relying only on prompts is a meaningful contribution.
    *   The Controllable Generation ensemble method based on text consistency is another contribution, addressing the answer format misalignment problem.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses relevant challenges in LLM-based OpenQA and demonstrates improvements on standard benchmarks.
    *   The experiments and ablation studies provide insights into the impact of knowledge quality and integration on model performance. Finding the linear relation between retrieval quality and the ability to accurately recall it is valuable for understanding the LLM.
    *   The out-of-domain and knowledge base independence tests further strengthen the framework's robustness.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem definition is clear, highlighting knowledge deficiency and answer format misalignment in LLMs.
    *   The GenKI framework is well-structured and motivated.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, covering various datasets, baselines, and ablation studies.
    *   The paper provides detailed implementation specifics, promoting reproducibility.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the components of GenKI are well-defined, it has some conceptual overlap with existing work in RAG and controlled generation. The novelty lies in the specific combinations and architectural choices, but the paper doesn't always clearly distinguish itself from related techniques.
    *   Some experimental results, like the weaker performance of LLaMA on MSMARCO compared to GLM, require more thorough analysis and justification.  It's mentioned this might be due to lack of supervised training, but this should be investigated further.
    *   The method of extracting the quality of the retrieval results seems to rely on frequency, which is unlikely to be an accurate estimation as frequency does not represent how relevant the extracted result is.
    *   The performance of the external retrieval (via the chatbot) is not high, which begs the question of whether there is a superior chatbot for the external retrieval module.

*   **Potential Impact:** The GenKI framework has the potential to influence the design of future OpenQA systems by emphasizing direct knowledge integration within LLM parameters.  The insights from the ablation studies can guide researchers in prioritizing different aspects of knowledge integration and controllable generation.

**Justification for Score**

The paper presents valuable contributions to the field of LLM-based OpenQA. The proposed framework directly addresses knowledge integration and answer format control which are prevalent issues in open-domain settings. Though some components draw inspiration from earlier research, the proposed architecture and design choices distinguish GenKI as an innovative method that leads to improvements over competitive benchmarks. Despite the presence of some minor shortcomings, such as the quality measurement of the retrieval and the external models selection, the contribution is substantial, and the ablation experiments are illuminating.

### A Comprehensive Real-World Assessment of Audio Watermarking Algorithms: Will They Survive Neural Codecs?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19663v1)
- **Authors**: Yigitcan Özer, Woosung Choi, Joan Serrà, Mayank Kumar Singh, Wei-Hsiang Liao, Yuki Mitsufuji
- **Abstract**: We present a framework to foster the evaluation of deep learning-based audio watermarking algorithms, establishing a standardized benchmark and allowing systematic comparisons. To simulate real-world usage, we introduce a comprehensive audio attack pipeline, featuring various distortions such as compression, background noise, and reverberation, and propose a diverse test dataset, including speech, environmental sounds, and music recordings. By assessing the performance of four existing watermarking algorithms on our framework, two main insights stand out: (i) neural compression techniques pose the most significant challenge, even when algorithms are trained with such compressions; and (ii) training with audio attacks generally improves robustness, although it is insufficient in some cases. Furthermore, we find that specific distortions, such as polarity inversion, time stretching, or reverb, seriously affect certain algorithms. Our contributions strengthen the robustness and perceptual assessment of audio watermarking algorithms across a wide range of applications, while ensuring a fair and consistent evaluation approach. The evaluation framework, including the attack pipeline, is accessible at github.com/SonyResearch/wm_robustness_eval.
- **Summary**: The paper introduces a comprehensive framework for evaluating the robustness and imperceptibility of deep learning-based audio watermarking algorithms. This framework includes a standardized benchmark, a diverse test dataset comprising speech, music, and environmental sounds, and a comprehensive audio attack pipeline featuring various distortions like compression, background noise, and reverberation. The authors evaluate four existing watermarking algorithms and analyze the impact of adversarial training on two of them. The findings reveal that neural compression techniques present the most significant challenge and that adversarial training improves robustness but doesn't fully resolve vulnerabilities. The authors also observe that certain distortions have a disproportionate impact on specific algorithms. The framework, including the attack pipeline, is made publicly available. The authors conclude that future work should focus on the trade-off between audio watermarking and neural codecs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the audio watermarking field by providing a standardized and comprehensive evaluation framework. The use of a diverse, high-fidelity dataset and a realistic attack pipeline improves upon previous benchmarking efforts like AudioMarkBench. The insights derived from evaluating existing algorithms are practically relevant, especially the observation that neural codecs present a major hurdle. The investigation into adversarial training reveals its benefits and limitations, contributing to a better understanding of how to improve robustness.

However, the paper has some weaknesses. The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary. While the framework is comprehensive, it's primarily an engineering effort to integrate existing datasets and attack methods. The analysis, while insightful, mainly confirms suspicions about the vulnerability of watermarking algorithms to compression and the benefits of adversarial training. The performance evaluations of only four algorithms could be viewed as a limited survey of the broader landscape of audio watermarking techniques. The "future work" point about the inherent competition between watermarking and neural codecs is not entirely new but the emphasis and discussion about this area contribute meaningfully to the discussion.

The significance lies in the practical utility of the framework. It allows researchers to rigorously evaluate and compare new algorithms, which could accelerate progress in the field. The findings provide valuable guidance for developing more robust watermarking techniques, particularly for applications where audio is processed using neural codecs. The public availability of the framework promotes reproducibility and further development by the community.

Given these considerations, the paper's contribution, while not groundbreaking, is solid and beneficial to the community, particularly in the areas of audio forensics and rights management. Its practical nature and thorough analysis outweigh its incremental novelty.

### LeCoDe: A Benchmark Dataset for Interactive Legal Consultation Dialogue Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19667v1)
- **Authors**: Weikang Yuan, Kaisong Song, Zhuoren Jiang, Junjie Cao, Yujie Zhang, Jun Lin, Kun Kuang, Ji Zhang, Xiaozhong Liu
- **Abstract**: Legal consultation is essential for safeguarding individual rights and ensuring access to justice, yet remains costly and inaccessible to many individuals due to the shortage of professionals. While recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) offer a promising path toward scalable, low-cost legal assistance, current systems fall short in handling the interactive and knowledge-intensive nature of real-world consultations. To address these challenges, we introduce LeCoDe, a real-world multi-turn benchmark dataset comprising 3,696 legal consultation dialogues with 110,008 dialogue turns, designed to evaluate and improve LLMs' legal consultation capability. With LeCoDe, we innovatively collect live-streamed consultations from short-video platforms, providing authentic multi-turn legal consultation dialogues. The rigorous annotation by legal experts further enhances the dataset with professional insights and expertise. Furthermore, we propose a comprehensive evaluation framework that assesses LLMs' consultation capabilities in terms of (1) clarification capability and (2) professional advice quality. This unified framework incorporates 12 metrics across two dimensions. Through extensive experiments on various general and domain-specific LLMs, our results reveal significant challenges in this task, with even state-of-the-art models like GPT-4 achieving only 39.8% recall for clarification and 59% overall score for advice quality, highlighting the complexity of professional consultation scenarios. Based on these findings, we further explore several strategies to enhance LLMs' legal consultation abilities. Our benchmark contributes to advancing research in legal domain dialogue systems, particularly in simulating more real-world user-expert interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LeCoDe: A Benchmark Dataset for Interactive Legal Consultation Dialogue Evaluation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LeCoDe, a new benchmark dataset for evaluating legal consultation dialogues. It consists of 3,696 real-world, multi-turn legal consultation dialogues extracted from live-streamed Q&A sessions on a short-video platform. These dialogues are annotated by legal experts, providing rich information about utterance intents, key facts (with importance scores), and advice summaries.  The paper also proposes a comprehensive evaluation framework encompassing clarification capabilities and advice quality. The authors perform extensive experiments using various LLMs, highlighting limitations in their performance in this complex task and proposing supervised fine-tuning strategies for improvement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies primarily in the data source and the multi-faceted evaluation framework. Using live-streamed legal consultations offers a level of realism that is often lacking in synthetic or single-turn datasets. The comprehensive evaluation framework, which includes clarification capability and advice quality (with fine-grained metrics), is a significant improvement over previous evaluations focused solely on final advice.

*   **Significance:** Legal consultation is an important application area for LLMs, and LeCoDe addresses a gap in publicly available datasets that can support this area. By establishing a benchmark and highlighting the shortcomings of existing models, the paper can potentially drive research towards better LLMs for legal assistance. The analysis of different fine-tuning strategies offers practical guidance for researchers.
    *   **Strengths:**
    *   **Authenticity and Realism:** The dataset is based on real-world legal consultations, capturing the nuances of human-expert interactions.
    *   **Comprehensive Annotation:** The rich annotation by legal experts provides valuable insights for training and evaluation.
    *   **Detailed Evaluation Framework:** The framework allows for a systematic and fine-grained assessment of LLMs' legal consultation capabilities.

    *   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Language Limitation:** The dataset is limited to Chinese consultations, which may limit the generalizability of findings.
    *   **Domain Specificity:** Legal consultation is a very specific task. Results may not generalize to other consultation or dialogue scenarios without further adaptation and validation.
    *   **User Simulation:** While the paper tests for the reliability of the user simulator, its performance will inevitably influence results. Using an LLM-based user simulator risks underestimating model performance, as an actual human user would exhibit a wider range of behaviors.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution with LeCoDe, which facilitates a more thorough and realistic evaluation of LLMs in legal consultation. The comprehensive annotation and evaluation framework are strengths that contribute to the paper's potential impact. However, the Chinese language limitation and domain specificity slightly reduce the paper's score.

### Burst Image Super-Resolution via Multi-Cross Attention Encoding and Multi-Scan State-Space Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19668v1)
- **Authors**: Tengda Huang, Yu Zhang, Tianren Li, Yufu Qu, Fulin Liu, Zhenzhong Wei
- **Abstract**: Multi-image super-resolution (MISR) can achieve higher image quality than single-image super-resolution (SISR) by aggregating sub-pixel information from multiple spatially shifted frames. Among MISR tasks, burst super-resolution (BurstSR) has gained significant attention due to its wide range of applications. Recent methods have increasingly adopted Transformers over convolutional neural networks (CNNs) in super-resolution tasks, due to their superior ability to capture both local and global context. However, most existing approaches still rely on fixed and narrow attention windows that restrict the perception of features beyond the local field. This limitation hampers alignment and feature aggregation, both of which are crucial for high-quality super-resolution. To address these limitations, we propose a novel feature extractor that incorporates two newly designed attention mechanisms: overlapping cross-window attention and cross-frame attention, enabling more precise and efficient extraction of sub-pixel information across multiple frames. Furthermore, we introduce a Multi-scan State-Space Module with the cross-frame attention mechanism to enhance feature aggregation. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world benchmarks demonstrate the superiority of our approach. Additional evaluations on ISO 12233 resolution test charts further confirm its enhanced super-resolution performance.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided document. Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel approach to burst image super-resolution (BurstSR) using a combination of multi-cross attention encoding and multi-scan state-space decoding. It addresses the limitations of existing methods that rely on fixed and narrow attention windows, hindering effective sub-pixel information extraction and feature aggregation. The proposed architecture incorporates: 1) a Multi-Cross Attention (MCA) module with overlapping Cross-Window Attention (CWA) and Cross-Frame Attention (CFA) to efficiently extract intra-frame features from multiple images, and 2) a Multi-Scan State-Space Module (MS-SSM) jointly used with CFA to mitigate misalignment introduced by deformable convolutional network (DCN)-based alignment. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, as well as ISO resolution test charts, demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method over existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of multi-cross attention encoding and multi-scan state-space decoding represents a meaningful advancement. While individual components like cross-attention and state-space models are not entirely new, their specific integration within a BurstSR pipeline, particularly the overlapping attention mechanism (CWA) and frame attention (CFA) *is* a novel contribution. The multi-scan approach to mitigate DCN misalignment is also innovative and potentially addresses a significant limitation of previous works. The contribution lies in the thoughtful design and integration of these components specifically for the BurstSR task, rather than introducing fundamentally new building blocks.

*   **Significance:** BurstSR is a practically relevant problem with applications in mobile photography, remote sensing, and video processing. Improving the quality of reconstructed images has direct real-world implications. The paper demonstrates tangible improvements over existing methods in terms of quantitative metrics (PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS) and qualitative visual results, particularly in preserving texture and structure in the reconstructed image. The results on the ISO resolution test chart provide evidence for enhanced ability to resolve fine details. The results for the synthesized dataset is convincing, but the performance gain in real-world scenarios appears more modest.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides thorough experimental results on both synthetic and real-world datasets, as well as the ISO resolution test charts. This lends credibility to the claims of superior performance.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches and explains how the proposed method addresses these limitations.
    *   **Well-Designed Architecture:** The proposed architecture is well-designed and justified. The individual components and their interactions are clearly explained.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study demonstrates the effectiveness of individual components within the architecture.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The proposed architecture is relatively complex, which may make it more difficult to implement and train. The parameter count is higher than certain competitive models (e.g., Burstormer). While the authors justify the complexity with performance gains, a discussion of computational efficiency beyond parameter count (e.g., inference time, memory footprint) would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Failure Cases:** While the paper highlights the strengths of the proposed method, it would be beneficial to discuss potential failure cases or limitations. For example, under what conditions might the multi-scan approach fail to adequately address DCN misalignment? Or, how robust is the method to varying levels of noise or blur in the input images?
    *   **The real-world improvements are modest:** The actual performance gained from the proposed method on real-world datasets is more modest than those gains achieved on synthetic datasets. The synthesized datasets used may not be representative of real-world image acquisitions, so further discussion and improvement may be necessary.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a new and effective approach to BurstSR. Other researchers may build upon the proposed architecture or adapt its components for other super-resolution tasks. The multi-scan state-space approach to address misalignment could be particularly influential.

**Overall:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of BurstSR. The proposed method demonstrably improves upon existing approaches, with well-justified architecture and thorough experimental results. While the architectural complexity and modest real-world gains are points to be considered, the paper is a worthwhile addition to the literature.

### Reshaping Representation Space to Balance the Safety and Over-rejection in Large Audio Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19670v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Yang, Lizhen Qu, Ehsan Shareghi, Gholamreza Haffari
- **Abstract**: Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) have extended the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) by enabling audio-based human interactions. However, recent research has revealed that LALMs remain vulnerable to harmful queries due to insufficient safety-alignment. Despite advances in defence measures for text and vision LLMs, effective safety-alignment strategies and audio-safety dataset specifically targeting LALMs are notably absent. Meanwhile defence measures based on Supervised Fine-tuning (SFT) struggle to address safety improvement while avoiding over-rejection issues, significantly compromising helpfulness. In this work, we propose an unsupervised safety-fine-tuning strategy as remedy that reshapes model's representation space to enhance existing LALMs safety-alignment while balancing the risk of over-rejection. Our experiments, conducted across three generations of Qwen LALMs, demonstrate that our approach significantly improves LALMs safety under three modality input conditions (audio-text, text-only, and audio-only) while increasing over-rejection rate by only 0.88% on average. Warning: this paper contains harmful examples.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the safety concerns in Large Audio Language Models (LALMs), which are vulnerable to harmful queries despite advances in defense measures for text and vision LLMs. The paper proposes a novel unsupervised safety-fine-tuning strategy called Reshaping Representation Space (RRS). RRS aims to enhance the safety alignment of LALMs while balancing the risk of over-rejection (falsely refusing benign queries). The method reshapes the model's representation space by identifying and localizing harmful and benign zones, improving safety without excessively compromising helpfulness. Experiments conducted on three generations of Qwen LALMs demonstrate that RRS significantly improves safety across different modality input conditions while only marginally increasing the over-rejection rate.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach to enhancing the safety of LALMs through unsupervised representation reshaping.  While safety fine-tuning is a common concept in LLMs, applying it specifically to LALMs and in an *unsupervised* manner to mitigate over-rejection is a relatively new idea.  The focus on reshaping the representation space, identifying "harmful" and "benign" zones, and then optimizing distances between these clusters is a valuable contribution.  The paper tackles a specific problem - lack of safety alignment datasets for LALMs - with an innovative solution.
*   **Significance:** The problem of ensuring the safety and alignment of LALMs is significant, especially with the increasing integration of audio interactions in human-machine interfaces. LALMs are becoming more prevalent in real-world applications, making the need for safety alignment strategies of utmost importance. Furthermore, the method addresses a key trade-off: improving safety without excessively diminishing usefulness.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the safety vulnerabilities of LALMs, and motivates the need for specific safety alignment strategies.
    *   **Novel Approach:** RRS offers a novel solution to safety fine-tuning by reshaping the representation space using an unsupervised approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of the RRS strategy on three LALM models (Qwen series) across different input modalities. It also compares it against several supervised fine-tuning strategies.
    *   **Balances Safety and Helpfulness:** The research explicitly addresses the trade-off between safety and over-rejection, which is a key issue in safety alignment.
    *   **Detailed Explanation:** The methodology, including the safety feature selection and fine-tuning protocol, is clearly explained with the right level of detail.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Existing Models and Datasets:**  The approach relies on existing LALM architectures (Qwen). While it addresses a crucial need of safety-tuning, it doesn't provide significant insights into designing safer LALM architectures from scratch. The creation of audio datasets from text datasets also presents limitations, as these might not fully capture the complexities of real-world audio scenarios. It may not generalise well with other types of audio models that are not based on LLMs.
    *   **Limited Scope of Harmful Queries:** The paper focuses on a specific type of harmful queries that can be refused. It doesn't address more nuanced types of harmful interactions, jailbreaking attempts, or adversarial attacks.
    *   **Over-rejection metric is based on Benign questions only.** The rejection rate is solely calculated based on benign queries. The analysis does not account for potential situations where genuinely harmful queries are incorrectly classified as answerable leading to potentially harmful responses. Thus, the safety metric lacks critical analysis in the context of how it affects overall model safety.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and effective strategy for safety-alignment of LALMs. The RRS strategy can serve as a baseline for future research in this area, and the insights gained from this study can guide the development of more robust safety alignment techniques. The focus on representation space manipulation could lead to a deeper understanding of model vulnerabilities and safety mechanisms.

**Justification for Score:**

I've assigned a score of 7.5 because the paper presents a significant contribution to the emerging field of LALM safety. The novel RRS strategy, and its specific focus on the unique challenges of LALMs makes a substantial contribution. However, the limitations need to be addressed through future work to increase the method's robustness.

### Comparing Moral Values in Western English-speaking societies and LLMs with Word Associations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19674v1)
- **Authors**: Chaoyi Xiang, Chunhua Liu, Simon De Deyne, Lea Frermann
- **Abstract**: As the impact of large language models increases, understanding the moral values they reflect becomes ever more important. Assessing the nature of moral values as understood by these models via direct prompting is challenging due to potential leakage of human norms into model training data, and their sensitivity to prompt formulation. Instead, we propose to use word associations, which have been shown to reflect moral reasoning in humans, as low-level underlying representations to obtain a more robust picture of LLMs' moral reasoning. We study moral differences in associations from western English-speaking communities and LLMs trained predominantly on English data. First, we create a large dataset of LLM-generated word associations, resembling an existing data set of human word associations. Next, we propose a novel method to propagate moral values based on seed words derived from Moral Foundation Theory through the human and LLM-generated association graphs. Finally, we compare the resulting moral conceptualizations, highlighting detailed but systematic differences between moral values emerging from English speakers and LLM associations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel method for comparing moral values in Western English-speaking societies and Large Language Models (LLMs) by analyzing word associations.  Instead of direct prompting, which can be unreliable, the authors generate word association graphs for both humans and LLMs. They then propagate moral values (based on Moral Foundations Theory) through these graphs and compare the resulting moral conceptualizations. The authors highlight differences and similarities in how LLMs and humans associate concepts, focusing on the role of emotionality, concreteness, and social context. The study demonstrates that LLMs capture some aspects of human moral understanding but also exhibit notable divergences, particularly regarding negative moral concepts and emotional grounding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its approach to assessing moral alignment. Using word associations to infer underlying moral reasoning, rather than relying on direct prompting, is a significant step forward. The global graph propagation method is also an improvement over previous approaches (like MAG) that rely on local subgraphs. The structural alignment of LLM and human word associations using specific metrics is also a novel and welcome addition.

*   **Significance:** Understanding the moral values encoded in LLMs is crucial for ensuring their safe and ethical deployment. The paper identifies key differences in how LLMs and humans process moral concepts, highlighting potential risks related to LLMs misinterpreting harmful situations due to a lack of affective and experiential grounding. This can inform future LLM design and training to better align with human moral understanding. The methodology can be used across languages and cultures to assess the morality embedded in a variety of large language models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Robust Methodology:** The use of word associations and graph propagation provides a more robust and less biased assessment of LLM morality compared to direct prompting. The paper carefully addresses issues of prompt sensitivity and data leakage.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper offers a detailed qualitative and quantitative analysis of the differences between human and LLM moral associations, going beyond simple accuracy measures.
    *   **Focus on Western Moral Values:** The paper appropriately focuses on Western moral values, given the data used for LLM training, and clearly states this limitation.
    *   **Clear Explanation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Cultural Specificity:** The study is limited to Western English-speaking cultures. While acknowledged by the authors, this significantly limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Reliance on Moral Foundations Theory:** The paper relies on Moral Foundations Theory (MFT), which, while widely used, is itself subject to debate and revision. The choice of this theoretical framework impacts which moral values are investigated, and it is unclear how general the findings are.
    *   **Indirect Inference:** While word associations provide a more robust measure than direct prompting, they still require interpretation and inference. It's possible that alternative interpretations of the observed differences exist.
    *   **Emphasis on Negative Concepts:** The results highlight that LLMs perform poorly for the negative, and a richer dataset with more diverse situations may need to be used in further experiments.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by providing a novel and more robust method for assessing moral alignment between LLMs and humans. It also identifies key areas where LLMs diverge from human moral understanding, particularly in the processing of negative concepts. The focus on cultural specificity is appropriate, but the limitations should be kept in mind. The reliance on MFT and the need for further validation are important considerations. Overall, the paper represents a valuable step toward ensuring the ethical deployment of LLMs.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Calibrating Pre-trained Language Classifiers on LLM-generated Noisy Labels via Iterative Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19675v1)
- **Authors**: Liqin Ye, Agam Shah, Chao Zhang, Sudheer Chava
- **Abstract**: The traditional process of creating labeled datasets is labor-intensive and expensive. Recent breakthroughs in open-source large language models (LLMs) have opened up a new avenue in generating labeled datasets automatically for various natural language processing (NLP) tasks, providing an alternative to such an expensive annotation process. However, the reliability of such auto-generated labels remains a significant concern due to inherent inaccuracies. When learning from noisy labels, the model's generalization is likely to be harmed as it is prone to overfit to those label noises. While previous studies in learning from noisy labels mainly focus on synthetic noise and real-world noise, LLM-generated label noise receives less attention. In this paper, we propose SiDyP: Simplex Label Diffusion with Dynamic Prior to calibrate the classifier's prediction, thus enhancing its robustness towards LLM-generated noisy labels. SiDyP retrieves potential true label candidates by neighborhood label distribution in text embedding space and iteratively refines noisy candidates using a simplex diffusion model. Our framework can increase the performance of the BERT classifier fine-tuned on both zero-shot and few-shot LLM-generated noisy label datasets by an average of 7.21% and 7.30% respectively. We demonstrate the effectiveness of SiDyP by conducting extensive benchmarking for different LLMs over a variety of NLP tasks. Our code is available on Github.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Calibrating Pre-trained Language Classifiers on LLM-generated Noisy Labels via Iterative Refinement" (SiDyP) addresses the problem of noisy labels generated by large language models (LLMs) when used for automatic data annotation. The authors argue that while LLMs offer a cost-effective alternative to traditional labeling methods, the inherent inaccuracies in LLM-generated labels can harm the generalization ability of models trained on this data. They propose a novel denoising approach called SiDyP, which uses a simplex label diffusion model to iteratively refine noisy labels. SiDyP retrieves potential true label candidates using neighborhood label distribution in text embedding space and progressively refines these candidates through a diffusion model conditioned on training dynamics. The paper presents extensive experimental results on various NLP tasks, LLMs, and types of noise, demonstrating the effectiveness of SiDyP in improving the robustness of classifiers trained on LLM-generated noisy labels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in addressing the specific problem of LLM-generated noisy labels. While learning from noisy labels is a well-studied area, the characteristics of LLM-generated noise are different from synthetic or real-world noise, making it a unique challenge. The authors design a denoising algorithm by first addressing the characteristics of LLM-generated noise. The specific approach of combining neighborhood label distribution with a simplex diffusion model for iterative refinement is also innovative. Previous work in label noise robustness relies on certain explicit or implicit noise assumptions, yet the authors specifically address the context-dependent nature of noise in LLM generated labels.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it tackles a practical problem that is becoming increasingly relevant as LLMs are more widely used for data annotation. If LLMs are to be trusted to label data for downstream training, it is important to have approaches like the authors' to tackle the problem of intrinsic noise. It presents a useful framework for enhancing the reliability of LLM-generated labels, which could have a significant impact on the efficiency and cost-effectiveness of NLP tasks. The experimental results show significant performance gains compared to strong baselines, highlighting the practical value of the proposed method. However, one weakness of the work is that many implementation choices are not discussed in-depth. For example, what are the trade-offs among various design choices for the distance matrix, how is the threshold value chosen, and how much does each candidate impact the final denoising? In addition, the method relies on fine-tuning existing pretrained methods, which can be computationally expensive. Furthermore, the reliance on the neighborhood structure assumes a certain level of consistency in label semantics and embedding structure, which might not hold in all cases. It would be good to clarify these limitations.

*   **Impact:** The paper is very likely to be well-received as an LLM generated noise method, which addresses a problem of growing importance. The extensive experiments and detailed analysis provide valuable insights into the characteristics of LLM-generated noise and the effectiveness of different denoising techniques. The code availability would improve adoption and further research.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty and practical significance of the paper in the context of increasingly prevalent LLM-generated noisy labels, its robust design, and strong experimental results, I assign a score of 7.5. While the method addresses a unique problem, it also builds upon previous work in noisy label learning and diffusion models. The weaknesses discussed above slightly lower the score. Despite this, the paper offers a novel and effective solution that could influence the development of more reliable and efficient NLP systems.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models' Reasoning Stalls: An Investigation into the Capabilities of Frontier Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19676v1)
- **Authors**: Lachlan McGinness, Peter Baumgartner
- **Abstract**: Empirical methods to examine the capability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to use Automated Theorem Prover (ATP) reasoning strategies are studied. We evaluate the performance of State of the Art models from December 2023 and August 2024 on PRONTOQA steamroller reasoning problems. For that, we develop methods for assessing LLM response accuracy and correct answer correlation. Our results show that progress in improving LLM reasoning abilities has stalled over the nine month period. By tracking completion tokens, we show that almost all improvement in reasoning ability since GPT-4 was released can be attributed to either hidden system prompts or the training of models to automatically use generic Chain of Thought prompting strategies. Among the ATP reasoning strategies tried, we found that current frontier LLMs are best able to follow the bottom-up (also known as forward-chaining) strategy. A low positive correlation was found between an LLM response containing correct reasoning and arriving at the correct conclusion.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Large Language Models' Reasoning Stalls: An Investigation into the Capabilities of Frontier Models" investigates the reasoning abilities of state-of-the-art Large Language Models (LLMs) using the PRONTOQA benchmark, focusing on deductive reasoning tasks. The authors evaluate models from December 2023 and August 2024, specifically GPT-3.5 Turbo (GPT3), GPT-4, GPT4-0, Google's Gemini-Pro, Claude 3 Opus, and Llama3.1 405B, employing in-context learning to teach them different Automated Theorem Prover (ATP) reasoning strategies (Bottom Up, Top Down, and Magic Set Transformation).  The research measures accuracy, token counts, and the faithfulness of the models to the prescribed reasoning strategies.  A key finding is that improvements in LLM reasoning over the nine-month period have largely stalled and can be attributed to built-in Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting or hidden system prompts. It also finds a weak correlation between correct reasoning and correct answers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Focus on Reasoning Process:** The paper's strength lies in its shift from merely measuring accuracy to assessing the soundness and faithfulness of LLM reasoning processes. This is important for building trustworthy AI systems.
    *   **Use of ATP Strategies:**  Employing ATP strategies as a framework for evaluating LLM reasoning is novel and insightful.  It provides a structured and well-defined approach to assess deductive capabilities.
    *   **Longitudinal Study:**  The comparison of models across a nine-month period provides valuable insights into the trajectory of LLM development and highlights the stagnation of core reasoning abilities.
    *   **ProntoQA Benchmark:** Using the PRONTOQA benchmark and its ability to generate problems reduces data contamination.
    *   **Attention to Detail:** The paper carefully considers the influence of implicit prompts.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study's reliance on a single benchmark (PRONTOQA) could limit the generalizability of the findings.  While ProntoQA is specifically designed for this type of analysis, results should be confirmed with other benchmarks.
    *   **Lack of Computational Analysis:** The paper acknowledges the absence of computational cost analysis.  This is a significant omission, as the efficiency of different reasoning strategies is a crucial consideration in real-world applications.
    *   **Specific LLMs:** Although the analysis includes LLMs, it would be more robust to include more open-source models.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper's focus on the *process* of reasoning, rather than just the outcome, provides critical insight into the limitations and nature of LLMs.
    *   The detection of stalled progress in reasoning improvement is an important contribution, prompting a re-evaluation of training strategies.
    *   The identification of implicit prompting mechanisms highlights the importance of transparency and control in LLM behavior.
    *   The weak correlation between correct reasoning and correct answers underscores the potential for LLMs to provide inaccurate or untrustworthy responses, despite achieving high accuracy on some tasks.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's findings could influence the direction of LLM research and development by:

*   Encouraging a greater emphasis on the development of *explicit* and *controllable* reasoning mechanisms.
*   Motivating research into more effective techniques for improving LLM reasoning abilities beyond simply scaling up model size or data.
*   Highlighting the importance of evaluating LLM reasoning processes as well as accuracy.
*   Advocating for greater transparency in the design and implementation of LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by critically examining the reasoning capabilities of LLMs. The focus on reasoning processes, the identification of stalled progress, and the detection of implicit prompting mechanisms are all significant findings. However, the study's limitations, namely its focus on a single benchmark and its lack of computational analysis, prevent it from receiving a higher score. Considering the paper's strengths and weaknesses, along with its potential influence on the field, I assign it a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deep Actor-Critics with Tight Risk Certificates
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19682v1)
- **Authors**: Bahareh Tasdighi, Manuel Haussmann, Yi-Shan Wu, Andres R. Masegosa, Melih Kandemir
- **Abstract**: After a period of research, deep actor-critic algorithms have reached a level where they influence our everyday lives. They serve as the driving force behind the continual improvement of large language models through user-collected feedback. However, their deployment in physical systems is not yet widely adopted, mainly because no validation scheme that quantifies their risk of malfunction. We demonstrate that it is possible to develop tight risk certificates for deep actor-critic algorithms that predict generalization performance from validation-time observations. Our key insight centers on the effectiveness of minimal evaluation data. Surprisingly, a small feasible of evaluation roll-outs collected from a pretrained policy suffices to produce accurate risk certificates when combined with a simple adaptation of PAC-Bayes theory. Specifically, we adopt a recently introduced recursive PAC-Bayes approach, which splits validation data into portions and recursively builds PAC-Bayes bounds on the excess loss of each portion's predictor, using the predictor from the previous portion as a data-informed prior. Our empirical results across multiple locomotion tasks and policy expertise levels demonstrate risk certificates that are tight enough to be considered for practical use.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces a method for generating risk certificates for deep actor-critic reinforcement learning algorithms. The core idea is to adapt the Recursive PAC-Bayes approach to RL, enabling the prediction of generalization performance based on a small set of validation rollouts from a pre-trained policy. The authors demonstrate empirically, across several locomotion tasks and policy expertise levels, that the resulting risk certificates are reasonably tight, suggesting their potential for practical use.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While PAC-Bayes theory has been applied to deep learning and, to a limited extent, in specific aspects of RL, the application of recursive PAC-Bayes, particularly in this adapted form, to provide tight risk certificates for deep actor-critic algorithms is novel. The focus on minimal evaluation data and the empirical demonstration of its effectiveness is also a valuable contribution. However, individual components like the REDQ algorithm or PAC-Bayes techniques are not new in isolation. The novelty lies in their combination and adaptation for this specific problem.
*   **Significance:** The ability to generate tight risk certificates for RL algorithms is significant because it addresses a critical barrier to the widespread adoption of RL in safety-critical applications. By providing a quantifiable measure of the algorithm's potential for malfunction, it can increase trust and confidence in the system's reliability. The empirical validation on locomotion tasks demonstrates the applicability of the approach to practical RL problems, although further validation on physical robots would significantly strengthen the significance. The method addresses a relevant problem, with sufficient empirical validation.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a critical challenge in RL: the lack of trustworthy validation schemes.
    *   Adapts a powerful learning-theoretic approach (Recursive PAC-Bayes) to the actor-critic setting.
    *   Empirically demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed method on standard locomotion tasks.
    *   Highlights the surprising effectiveness of minimal evaluation data, which increases practical applicability.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The empirical evaluation is limited to simulated environments using MuJoCo. While standard, these environments are still simplifications of the real world. Demonstrating the approach on physical robots would be a significant step forward.
    *   The selection of REDQ is somewhat arbitrary. While the paper mentions this choice is pragmatic rather than restrictive, expanding the evaluation to other actor-critic algorithms could be beneficial.
    *   While the results are promising, the "tightness" of the risk certificates is relative. It's not clear if the level of tightness achieved is sufficient for truly safety-critical deployments. More detailed comparisons to alternative bounding techniques would be useful.
    *   The explanation of the technical details (e.g., data splitting, prior selection) is somewhat terse and assumes familiarity with PAC-Bayes theory.
    *   The work focuses primarily on *evaluating* the risk, rather than directly *training* a policy under a risk constraint. A natural next step is to develop PAC-Bayes-constrained training for RL agents.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of reinforcement learning by adapting Recursive PAC-Bayes to generate risk certificates for deep actor-critic algorithms. While it has limitations, particularly the reliance on simulated environments and a single RL algorithm, the results are promising and address a crucial challenge in the trustworthy deployment of RL systems. The potential impact on safety-critical applications justifies a relatively high score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models for Planning: A Comprehensive and Systematic Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19683v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Cao, Tianyi Men, Wencan Liu, Jingwen Zhang, Xuzhao Li, Xixun Lin, Dianbo Sui, Yanan Cao, Kang Liu, Jun Zhao
- **Abstract**: Planning represents a fundamental capability of intelligent agents, requiring comprehensive environmental understanding, rigorous logical reasoning, and effective sequential decision-making. While Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable performance on certain planning tasks, their broader application in this domain warrants systematic investigation. This paper presents a comprehensive review of LLM-based planning. Specifically, this survey is structured as follows: First, we establish the theoretical foundations by introducing essential definitions and categories about automated planning. Next, we provide a detailed taxonomy and analysis of contemporary LLM-based planning methodologies, categorizing them into three principal approaches: 1) External Module Augmented Methods that combine LLMs with additional components for planning, 2) Finetuning-based Methods that involve using trajectory data and feedback signals to adjust LLMs in order to improve their planning abilities, and 3) Searching-based Methods that break down complex tasks into simpler components, navigate the planning space, or enhance decoding strategies to find the best solutions. Subsequently, we systematically summarize existing evaluation frameworks, including benchmark datasets, evaluation metrics and performance comparisons between representative planning methods. Finally, we discuss the underlying mechanisms enabling LLM-based planning and outline promising research directions for this rapidly evolving field. We hope this survey will serve as a valuable resource to inspire innovation and drive progress in this field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a comprehensive survey of research on using Large Language Models (LLMs) for planning. It categorizes LLM-based planning approaches into three main types: (1) External Module Augmented Methods (integrating LLMs with external components like planners or memory), (2) Finetuning-based Methods (enhancing planning by finetuning LLMs on trajectory or feedback data), and (3) Searching-based Methods (decomposing tasks, exploring the planning space, or improving decoding strategies).  The survey also includes a systematic review of existing datasets, evaluation metrics, and performance comparisons. Finally, the paper discusses the underlying mechanisms enabling LLM-based planning and outlines promising research directions.  The goal is to offer a valuable resource to inspire innovation and drive progress in the field.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The survey covers a wide range of literature related to LLM-based planning, providing a holistic view of the field. The categorization of planning approaches is well-defined and useful for structuring the landscape.
    *   **Systematic Organization:** The paper is well-organized, covering definitions, methods, evaluation, and analysis, and proposing future directions.
    *   **Practical Guidance:** The analysis of strengths and limitations of different methods offers practical guidance for researchers.
    *   **Open Resources:** The availability of a curated GitHub repository with planning papers is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Up-to-date:** While surveys are immediately somewhat outdated, the inclusion of work from 2025 is good as of time of the document's creation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Depth in Mechanistic Interpretations:**  While the paper mentions underlying mechanisms of LLM planning, the section could benefit from greater depth and critical analysis. What are the actual mechanisms that are enabling LLM-based planning?
    *   **Uneven Coverage of Datasets and Scenarios:**  The paper mentions different scenarios, but the analysis of datasets within each scenario may be uneven, potentially overlooking nuances and specific challenges associated with certain tasks.
    *   **Rapidly Evolving Field:** The field of LLMs is moving extremely quickly. Even with its recent publication date, it will require frequent updates to remain current. There's a risk of quickly becoming a snapshot rather than a consistently updated guide. The survey will become less useful as LLM development progresses.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper contributes a systematic organization and categorization of a rapidly growing area of research, which is helpful for both newcomers and experienced researchers.  The identification of key research challenges and promising directions can guide future work.
    *   The comprehensiveness distinguishes it from existing surveys which focus on specific aspects or have a narrower scope.
    *   However, the core contribution is primarily *surveying* existing work.  While valuable, it doesn't introduce fundamentally new concepts or techniques. The paper itself could be seen as incremental work to organize and synthesize knowledge in the area.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper has the potential to be a widely cited reference for researchers entering the field of LLM-based planning.
    *   It can influence the direction of future research by highlighting key limitations and open challenges.
    *   Its influence will depend on how actively the authors maintain and update the survey to keep pace with the field's rapid development.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper is a valuable and well-executed survey, but its novelty is limited. While the comprehensive scope and systematic approach are significant, it primarily synthesizes existing knowledge rather than introducing groundbreaking concepts or methodologies. The identified research directions are insightful, but not entirely unexpected given the current trends. There are very few new insights the reviewers find. As such a high score is not warranted as this is primarily a survey.
