### ReChisel: Effective Automatic Chisel Code Generation by LLM with Reflection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19734v1)
- **Authors**: Juxin Niu, Xiangfeng Liu, Dan Niu, Xi Wang, Zhe Jiang, Nan Guan
- **Abstract**: Coding with hardware description languages (HDLs) such as Verilog is a time-intensive and laborious task. With the rapid advancement of large language models (LLMs), there is increasing interest in applying LLMs to assist with HDL coding. Recent efforts have demonstrated the potential of LLMs in translating natural language to traditional HDL Verilog. Chisel, a next-generation HDL based on Scala, introduces higher-level abstractions, facilitating more concise, maintainable, and scalable hardware designs. However, the potential of using LLMs for Chisel code generation remains largely unexplored. This work proposes ReChisel, an LLM-based agentic system designed to enhance the effectiveness of Chisel code generation. ReChisel incorporates a reflection mechanism to iteratively refine the quality of generated code using feedback from compilation and simulation processes, and introduces an escape mechanism to break free from non-progress loops. Experiments demonstrate that ReChisel significantly improves the success rate of Chisel code generation, achieving performance comparable to state-of-the-art LLM-based agentic systems for Verilog code generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ReChisel: Effective Automatic Chisel Code Generation by LLM with Reflection."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReChisel, an LLM-based agentic system designed to improve the automatic generation of Chisel code from natural language specifications. Recognizing the limitations of directly using LLMs for Chisel due to the language's relative novelty and smaller codebase compared to Verilog, ReChisel employs a reflection mechanism.  This involves iteratively refining the generated code using feedback from compilation and simulation. The system also incorporates an "escape mechanism" to break free from non-progress loops, where the LLM gets stuck repeating the same errors.  The authors demonstrate that ReChisel significantly improves the success rate of Chisel code generation, achieving performance comparable to state-of-the-art LLM-based agentic systems for Verilog.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper demonstrates novelty in several ways:
1.  *Application to Chisel:* It directly addresses the relatively unexplored area of Chisel code generation by LLMs.  Existing work has primarily focused on Verilog.
2.  *Reflection Mechanism:* While reflection is used in LLM-assisted code generation, the paper tailors it to the specific challenges of Chisel. Using compiler feedback is useful, but not groundbreaking.
3.  *Escape Mechanism:* Addressing the issue of "non-progress loops" is a valuable addition.  This highlights a practical problem in LLM-based iterative code improvement and offers a solution. This component is relatively novel.
4. *Benchmarks:* The paper tests and reports results using a variety of benchmarks.

*Significance:*

The significance of the work lies in:
1.  *Enabling Chisel for LLM-assisted Design:*  If ReChisel proves robust, it could make Chisel a more accessible language for hardware designers, especially those without extensive Chisel experience. This promotes the use of a modern HDL.
2.  *Improved Productivity:* Automatic code generation has the potential to significantly reduce the time and effort required for hardware design.
3.  *Generalizability:* The reflection and escape mechanisms developed in ReChisel might be applicable to other domains of LLM-assisted code generation.
4. *Comparison to Verilog*: The work provides a helpful comparison between using LLMs to generate Chisel code versus Verilog.

*Strengths:*
*   *Well-Defined Problem:* The paper clearly identifies the challenge of Chisel code generation with LLMs.
*   *Effective Solution:* The reflection and escape mechanisms are logical and well-integrated.
*   *Experimental Validation:*  The paper provides comprehensive experimental results across several benchmarks and LLMs, demonstrating the effectiveness of ReChisel. It also considers the impact of the number of iterations.
* *Case Study*: The paper provides a concrete example to show the reflection mechanism in action.

*Weaknesses:*

*   *Limited Scope:* The evaluation is limited to module-level code generation. The applicability to larger, more complex systems is not demonstrated.
*   *Dependence on LLM Capabilities:* The performance of ReChisel is inherently limited by the capabilities of the underlying LLM. This means that advances in LLMs will directly translate to improvements in ReChisel, but also that ReChisel's usefulness is tied to the continued progress of LLMs.
* *Limited Comparison*: There is limited comparison to other agentic systems (only AutoChip) and there is no ablation study conducted to evaluate the contribution of each component (e.g. reflection versus escape mechanism).
* *Error Type Identification*: The error types identified are rather simple and might not be relevant for more complicated projects.

*Potential Influence:*

ReChisel has the potential to influence the field by:
1.  *Encouraging More Research:* It highlights Chisel as a relevant target for LLM-assisted design, potentially leading to further research.
2.  *Informing the Design of Future Systems:* The reflection and escape mechanisms could be adopted and improved upon in future LLM-based code generation systems.
3.  *Practical Tool:* ReChisel can serve as a practical tool for hardware designers using Chisel.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper addresses a relevant problem, provides a sensible and effective solution, and backs it up with good experimental validation. The "escape mechanism" is a noteworthy contribution. The main limitation is that the core idea of using compiler feedback for iterative code improvement is not radically new and that the evaluation is primarily at the module level, leaving questions about its applicability to larger designs.  The paper is a solid contribution to the field, and could influence future work on LLM-assisted hardware design using Chisel.

### Token-level Accept or Reject: A Micro Alignment Approach for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19743v1)
- **Authors**: Yang Zhang, Yu Yu, Bo Tang, Yu Zhu, Chuxiong Sun, Wenqiang Wei, Jie Hu, Zipeng Xie, Zhiyu Li, Feiyu Xiong, Edward Chung
- **Abstract**: With the rapid development of Large Language Models (LLMs), aligning these models with human preferences and values is critical to ensuring ethical and safe applications. However, existing alignment techniques such as RLHF or DPO often require direct fine-tuning on LLMs with billions of parameters, resulting in substantial computational costs and inefficiencies. To address this, we propose Micro token-level Accept-Reject Aligning (MARA) approach designed to operate independently of the language models. MARA simplifies the alignment process by decomposing sentence-level preference learning into token-level binary classification, where a compact three-layer fully-connected network determines whether candidate tokens are "Accepted" or "Rejected" as part of the response. Extensive experiments across seven different LLMs and three open-source datasets show that MARA achieves significant improvements in alignment performance while reducing computational costs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Micro token-level Accept-Reject Aligning (MARA), a novel approach to aligning Large Language Models (LLMs) with human preferences and values. Unlike traditional alignment methods like RLHF and DPO, which require fine-tuning the entire LLM, MARA operates independently by decomposing sentence-level preference learning into token-level binary classification. A compact, three-layer fully-connected network determines whether candidate tokens are "Accepted" or "Rejected" during the response generation process. The paper demonstrates, through extensive experiments across various LLMs and datasets, that MARA achieves significant improvements in alignment performance while substantially reducing computational costs. A key contribution is the decoupling of the alignment model from the underlying language model, allowing for greater flexibility and transferability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the *decoupled* and *micro token-level* approach to LLM alignment.  While token-level alignment isn't entirely new (as the paper itself notes), the combination of decoupling this process entirely from the LLM, using a small, efficient network, and framing it as a binary "accept/reject" decision is a significant departure from existing paradigms. The Aligner work cited is concurrent, but MARA distinguishes itself by the scale and methodology of the alignment model.

*   **Significance:** The significance of MARA stems from its potential to democratize LLM alignment. The substantial reduction in computational cost addresses a significant barrier to entry for researchers and practitioners with limited resources. The demonstrated transferability of alignment models across different LLMs is also crucial for practical applications, as it allows for greater flexibility and efficiency.  If the claimed performance holds up under broader scrutiny, MARA could become a valuable tool for ensuring the ethical and safe deployment of LLMs. However, further exploration of possible side effects of such a fine grained control method is necessary.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Computational Efficiency:**  The most compelling strength is the reduction in computational cost. The use of a small, three-layer network for alignment is a significant advantage.
    *   **Transferability:** The ability to transfer alignment models across different LLMs is a valuable asset.
    *   **Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements in alignment performance compared to existing methods, as claimed.
    *   **Transparency and Reproducibility:** Releasing the complete implementation source code enhances the credibility of the work and facilitates future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reward Model Dependency:**  The approach still relies on a reward model, which needs to be trained. The training of this reward model is still a substantial upfront investment, even if smaller than fine-tuning the LLM itself. The potential bias inherent in the reward model could also affect the alignment performance.
    *   **Limited Evaluation on larger models:** The lack of experimental results with Llama-3-70B and other very large models is a limitation, as it is crucial to ensure the scalability of MARA to these more powerful LLMs.
    *   **Limited types of evaluation:** The metric presented in the paper is a preference rate, which is insufficient for assessing the qualities like fluency, coherency, or other side effect introduced by the presented approach.
    *   **Reliance on Supervised Fine Tuning**: The approach requires the SFT language model, and does not describe how does the authors tackle the possible information collapse as described by other methods.

*   **Potential Influence:**  MARA has the potential to influence the field by providing a more efficient and accessible way to align LLMs. It could also inspire new research into decoupled alignment approaches.

*   **Justification for Score:** Given the novelty in decoupling and efficiency, good experimental validation, and potential impact on accessibility, I assign a score that reflects the weaknesses in assessment and reliance on components that are still trained at high computational cost.
    Therefore:
**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SAIL: Self-supervised Albedo Estimation from Real Images with a Latent Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19751v1)
- **Authors**: Hala Djeghim, Nathan Piasco, Luis Roldão, Moussab Bennehar, Dzmitry Tsishkou, Céline Loscos, Désiré Sidibé
- **Abstract**: Intrinsic image decomposition aims at separating an image into its underlying albedo and shading components, isolating the base color from lighting effects to enable downstream applications such as virtual relighting and scene editing. Despite the rise and success of learning-based approaches, intrinsic image decomposition from real-world images remains a significant challenging task due to the scarcity of labeled ground-truth data. Most existing solutions rely on synthetic data as supervised setups, limiting their ability to generalize to real-world scenes. Self-supervised methods, on the other hand, often produce albedo maps that contain reflections and lack consistency under different lighting conditions. To address this, we propose SAIL, an approach designed to estimate albedo-like representations from single-view real-world images. We repurpose the prior knowledge of a latent diffusion model for unconditioned scene relighting as a surrogate objective for albedo estimation. To extract the albedo, we introduce a novel intrinsic image decomposition fully formulated in the latent space. To guide the training of our latent diffusion model, we introduce regularization terms that constrain both the lighting-dependent and independent components of our latent image decomposition. SAIL predicts stable albedo under varying lighting conditions and generalizes to multiple scenes, using only unlabeled multi-illumination data available online.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and am ready to provide a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary**

The paper introduces SAIL, a self-supervised approach for estimating albedo (base color, without lighting) from single real-world images.  The key idea is to repurpose a pre-trained latent diffusion model, typically used for generating images, for unconditioned scene relighting. This is used as a surrogate task for albedo estimation, allowing the system to learn without requiring ground-truth albedo labels.  SAIL performs a novel intrinsic image decomposition fully within the latent space of the diffusion model, explicitly separating lighting-invariant (albedo) and lighting-dependent components. Regularization terms are added in the latent space to encourage consistent albedo estimates under varying lighting conditions. Experiments on public datasets demonstrate that SAIL outperforms supervised and self-supervised baselines in terms of robustness and albedo quality, generalizing well to out-of-domain data and multiple scenes, utilizing only time-lapse images data available online.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*

The paper has several components that contribute to its novelty:

1.  **Self-Supervised Albedo Estimation with Latent Diffusion:** Utilizing a pre-trained diffusion model for albedo estimation in a self-supervised manner is a significant departure from traditional supervised methods that rely on synthetic data or limited real-world datasets with ground truth.  Leveraging the strong priors embedded in latent diffusion models for this specific task is a novel application of these models. This approach addresses a crucial problem within inverse rendering, by tackling the data scarcity of labelled intrinsic images for real world scenes.

2.  **Latent Space Intrinsic Decomposition:**  Performing the entire intrinsic image decomposition process (separating albedo and shading) within the latent space of a diffusion model is a crucial innovation. It takes advantage of the expressive power and learned structure of the latent space, potentially leading to more robust and consistent decompositions. Furthermore, the formulation of the lighting representation at a higher level of abstraction in latent space compared to image-space methods is a valuable and novel idea.

3.  **Regularization for Albedo Consistency:** The introduction of regularization terms (latent albedo regularization, cross-consistency, and lighting component regularization) specifically designed to guide the training towards more consistent and meaningful albedo estimates is a key contribution. These regularization terms address the inherent ambiguity in intrinsic image decomposition and ensure that the model learns a physically plausible albedo representation.

4. **Utilization of internet time-lapse sequences**: The paper uses unlabeled multi-illumination data from online, this is a novel strategy and improves the paper's significance.

*Significance and Impact:*

The paper's potential significance lies in several areas:

1.  **Improved Albedo Estimation:** The empirical results suggest that SAIL produces high-quality albedo estimates that are more consistent and robust than existing methods, especially in challenging real-world scenarios. Improved albedo estimation can benefit a wide range of applications, including virtual relighting, scene editing, and 3D reconstruction.

2.  **Reduced Reliance on Labeled Data:**  The self-supervised nature of SAIL significantly reduces the need for labeled data, which is often expensive and time-consuming to acquire.  This makes it more practical to apply intrinsic image decomposition to a wider range of real-world scenes.

3.  **New Research Directions:** The paper opens up new research directions in the field of intrinsic image decomposition, particularly in exploring the use of latent diffusion models and self-supervised learning techniques.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear problem statement and well-defined solution.**
*   **Novel and effective approach based on latent diffusion models.**
*   **Thorough experimental evaluation on multiple datasets.**
*   **Qualitative results that demonstrate the superior performance of SAIL.**
*   **Comprehensive ablation study that validates the contribution of individual components.**
*   **Well-written and organized paper.**

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Dependence on Pre-trained Diffusion Model:** SAIL relies on a pre-trained diffusion model, which might limit its applicability if a suitable pre-trained model is not available for a specific domain or data distribution. The performance of SAIL may also be affected by the quality and characteristics of the pre-trained model.
*   **Computational Cost:**  Training diffusion models can be computationally expensive. While the paper reports training time, it would be helpful to have a more detailed analysis of the computational cost of SAIL, including both training and inference.
*   **Qualitative Limitations:** While the qualitative results are generally strong, some lighting artifacts and shadows still remain in the predicted albedos. This suggests that there is room for further improvement in the model's ability to separate lighting effects from albedo.

*Score Justification:*

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I believe the paper deserves a score of **8**.

*Rationale:*

The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to albedo estimation, leveraging the power of latent diffusion models in a self-supervised setting. The latent space intrinsic decomposition and the carefully designed regularization terms are significant contributions that address key challenges in the field. The experimental results demonstrate the superior performance of SAIL compared to existing methods. The paper is well-written and provides a thorough analysis of the proposed approach. However, the dependence on a pre-trained diffusion model, potential computational cost, and remaining limitations in fully removing lighting artifacts prevent it from achieving a higher score. It's a strong, innovative contribution that is likely to influence future research in intrinsic image decomposition and inverse rendering.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Divide and Conquer: Grounding LLMs as Efficient Decision-Making Agents via Offline Hierarchical Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19761v1)
- **Authors**: Zican Hu, Wei Liu, Xiaoye Qu, Xiangyu Yue, Chunlin Chen, Zhi Wang, Yu Cheng
- **Abstract**: While showing sophisticated reasoning abilities, large language models (LLMs) still struggle with long-horizon decision-making tasks due to deficient exploration and long-term credit assignment, especially in sparse-reward scenarios. Inspired by the divide-and-conquer principle, we propose an innovative framework **GLIDER** (**G**rounding **L**anguage Models as Eff**I**cient **D**ecision-Making Agents via Offline Hi**E**rarchical **R**einforcement Learning) that introduces a parameter-efficient and generally applicable hierarchy to LLM policies. We develop a scheme where the low-level controller is supervised with abstract, step-by-step plans that are learned and instructed by the high-level policy. This design decomposes complicated problems into a series of coherent chain-of-thought reasoning sub-tasks, providing flexible temporal abstraction to significantly enhance exploration and learning for long-horizon tasks. Furthermore, GLIDER facilitates fast online adaptation to non-stationary environments owing to the strong transferability of its task-agnostic low-level skills. Experiments on ScienceWorld and ALFWorld benchmarks show that GLIDER achieves consistent performance gains, along with enhanced generalization capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GLIDER, a novel offline hierarchical reinforcement learning framework designed to improve the decision-making capabilities of large language models (LLMs). GLIDER addresses the challenges of long-horizon tasks, deficient exploration, and sparse rewards by implementing a two-level hierarchical policy.  A high-level policy learns abstract, step-by-step plans, which are then executed by a low-level controller. This decomposition facilitates exploration and credit assignment. The low-level controller is trained using behavior cloning and then refined using offline RL. The paper demonstrates that GLIDER achieves consistent performance gains and enhanced generalization on ScienceWorld and ALFWorld benchmarks, and also shows the method can quickly adapt to new environments via online fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of using hierarchical RL with LLMs isn't entirely new. Previous works have explored similar avenues. However, GLIDER's novelty lies in its specific implementation, parameter-efficient design (sharing parameters and hierarchical prompts), and the particular combination of offline RL techniques. The emphasis on developing generalizable low-level skills and the specific offline-to-online adaptation strategy also contribute to the novelty. The design choice to use environment observations to dictate sub-task completion eliminates the dependence on any manual or task-specific design, which enhances general applicability.

* **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial problem in the field: making LLMs more effective decision-making agents, particularly in complex and sparse-reward environments. If GLIDER's approach proves robust and scalable, it could have a significant impact on the development of more capable AI agents. The benefits of online adaptation are another promising contribution, particularly in real-world settings where environments are frequently non-stationary. The comprehensive evaluation, including ablation studies and generalization analysis, strengthens the claims.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   The hierarchical design is intuitively appealing for complex tasks.
    *   The parameter-efficient architecture is beneficial for practical applications.
    *   The offline-to-online adaptation addresses a key challenge in real-world deployment.
    *   The extensive experimental results on relevant benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of the method.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   The analysis of data mixture ratios demonstrates the robustness of method in face of varying data qualities.
    *   Detailed algorithm pseudocode makes it relatively straightforward to reproduce.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies on existing techniques (behavior cloning, offline RL). While the combination and specific design choices contribute to the novelty, the core components aren't groundbreaking on their own.
    *   The benchmarks, while standard, have limitations in terms of real-world complexity. It's unclear how well GLIDER would generalize to more complex, unstructured environments.
    *   The claim of parameter efficiency could be further supported by comparison to fine-tuning baselines that also use parameter-efficient techniques like LoRA.
    *   The reliance on a GPT-4 model to decompose demonstration data into subtasks may limit the widespread adoption. Future directions should focus on eliminating the dependence on external models.

*   **Potential Influence:** GLIDER has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and effective framework for grounding LLMs as decision-making agents. The ideas of hierarchical RL, generalizable low-level skills, and offline-to-online adaptation could inspire future research. It can push researchers to explore task-agnostic low-level skill developments and also to ground LLMs to handle non-stationary environments. The experimental results serve as a strong baseline for future work in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty (specific implementation over core algorithmic advances) and significance (addressing a critical problem with strong empirical results), a score of 7 is appropriate. While the core RL techniques are not fundamentally new, the carefully designed architecture, parameter sharing, and emphasis on generalization and adaptability make this a valuable contribution that is likely to influence future research. The weaknesses related to benchmark complexity and reliance on GPT-4 limit the impact somewhat, but the solid empirical results and clear articulation of the method justify a relatively high score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Agentic Predictor: Performance Prediction for Agentic Workflows via Multi-View Encoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19764v1)
- **Authors**: Patara Trirat, Wonyong Jeong, Sung Ju Hwang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable capabilities across diverse tasks, but optimizing LLM-based agentic systems remains challenging due to the vast search space of agent configurations, prompting strategies, and communication patterns. Existing approaches often rely on heuristic-based tuning or exhaustive evaluation, which can be computationally expensive and suboptimal. This paper proposes Agentic Predictor, a lightweight predictor for efficient agentic workflow evaluation. Agentic Predictor is equipped with a multi-view workflow encoding technique that leverages multi-view representation learning of agentic systems by incorporating code architecture, textual prompts, and interaction graph features. To achieve high predictive accuracy while significantly reducing the number of required workflow evaluations for training a predictor, Agentic Predictor employs cross-domain unsupervised pretraining. By learning to approximate task success rates, Agentic Predictor enables fast and accurate selection of optimal agentic workflow configurations for a given task, significantly reducing the need for expensive trial-and-error evaluations. Experiments on a carefully curated benchmark spanning three domains show that our predictor outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both predictive accuracy and workflow utility, highlighting the potential of performance predictors in streamlining the design of LLM-based agentic workflows.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Agentic Predictor: Performance Prediction for Agentic Workflows via Multi-View Encoding" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Agentic Predictor, a framework for efficiently predicting the performance of agentic workflows (systems built using LLMs for automated task execution). The core idea is to learn to predict workflow performance *before* actually running the expensive workflows, saving computational resources. It tackles the problem of agentic workflow heterogeneity using a multi-view encoding technique (graphs, code, prompts) and addresses the scarcity of labeled data via cross-domain unsupervised pretraining. Experiments on a curated benchmark demonstrate improvements in both prediction accuracy and workflow utility compared to baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a significant problem:** Optimizing agentic workflows is a challenging bottleneck due to the combinatorial explosion of possible configurations and the high cost of evaluating each one. This paper directly tackles this.
    *   **Multi-View Encoding:** The idea of encoding agentic workflows using multiple perspectives (graph structure, code, prompts) is well-motivated and likely to capture more nuanced features compared to single-representation approaches.
    *   **Cross-Domain Pretraining:**  The paper effectively addresses the issue of limited labeled data by leveraging unsupervised pretraining on related agentic tasks.  This is a practical and valuable contribution.
    *   **Solid Experimental Evaluation:** The paper provides a fairly comprehensive experimental evaluation on a reasonably well-established benchmark (FLORA-Bench). The use of multiple datasets across several domains strengthens the validity of the results. Ablation studies clearly support the design choices.
    *   **Search-Agnostic:** The design of the model being search-agnostic allows for integration with other search-based paradigms.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the *combination* of techniques is novel, the individual components (multi-view representation learning, unsupervised pretraining, using predictors for NAS) are each relatively well-established in their respective fields. The architectural innovation isn't groundbreaking.
    *   **Limited Workflow Optimization:** The paper claims to improve workflow utility but focuses more on the prediction aspect than on showcasing the actual *optimization* process. A more in-depth analysis of the effectiveness in a real workflow optimization scenario would be better. The optimization is primarily random search guided by the predictor, which might not be the most sophisticated.
    *   **Dependence on FLORA-Bench:** The evaluation heavily relies on a single benchmark (FLORA-Bench), especially the AFlow subset. While this provides a standardized comparison, it limits the scope of the evaluation and raises questions about generalizability to other agentic workflow domains or tasks.  Ideally, they would have included a few novel/custom benchmarks to demonstrate wider applicability.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The work is significant as it presents a practically useful method for accelerating the design of agentic workflows.
    *   It could lead to more efficient exploration of the design space and help practitioners build more effective LLM-based agentic systems.
    *   By reducing reliance on expensive LLM API calls, it also makes agentic workflows more accessible and cost-effective.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper addresses a real problem with a reasonable approach, the novelty is somewhat limited. The core ideas aren't revolutionary, but their combination in the context of agentic workflows is valuable. The experimental results are convincing within the constraints of the chosen benchmark. The paper would be stronger with more innovative architectural designs and a more rigorous analysis of the workflow optimization capabilities.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### On some coupled local and nonlocal diffusion models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19765v1)
- **Authors**: Juan Pablo Borthagaray, Patrick Ciarlet Jr
- **Abstract**: We study problems in which a local model is coupled with a nonlocal one. We propose two energies: both of them are based on the same classical weighted $H^1$-semi norm to model the local part, while two different weighted $H^s$-semi norms, with $s \in (0,1)$, are used to model the nonlocal part. The corresponding strong formulations are derived. In doing so, one needs to develop some technical tools, such as suitable integration by parts formulas for operators with variable diffusivity, and one also needs to study the mapping properties of the Neumann operators that arise. In contrast to problems coupling purely local models, in which one requires transmission conditions on the interface between the subdomains, the presence of a nonlocal operator may give rise to nonlocal fluxes. These nonlocal fluxes may enter the problem as a source term, thereby changing its structure. Finally, we focus on a specific problem, that we consider most relevant, and study regularity of solutions and finite element discretizations. We provide numerical experiments to illustrate the most salient features of the models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the coupling of local and nonlocal diffusion models. Two different energy formulations are presented, both incorporating a classical weighted H¹-seminorm for the local component and distinct weighted H³-seminorms (where s is between 0 and 1) for the nonlocal part. The authors derive the corresponding strong formulations, requiring the development of specific technical tools, including integration by parts formulas for operators with variable diffusivity and an analysis of Neumann operator mapping properties. The authors highlight that unlike purely local couplings, the presence of a nonlocal operator can lead to nonlocal fluxes that influence the problem structure. The paper concentrates on a particular relevant problem, analyzes solution regularity, and explores finite element discretizations. Numerical experiments are performed to demonstrate key model characteristics. The study also delves into transmission conditions between local and nonlocal parts, investigating when classical conditions can be used.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic exploration of *different* ways to couple local and nonlocal diffusion models through an energy-based approach.  While the concept of coupling such models isn't entirely new (as the authors acknowledge in the reference to Acosta et al.), the introduction of *two distinct* energy formulations, the detailed derivation of strong forms, and the rigorous analysis of transmission conditions contribute meaningfully to the field.  The focus on the impact of nonlocal fluxes is a significant point of departure from previous works that primarily dealt with purely local models.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work rests on its practical implications for problems involving both local and nonlocal interactions, like fracture mechanics or diffusion processes in heterogeneous media. Understanding the transmission conditions and regularity properties of the coupled system is crucial for accurate numerical simulations. The convergence analysis for finite element discretizations ensures reliability when implementing the models. The comparison of different coupling strategies helps to select the best approach for a particular situation. The attention given to the case where *s* is less than 1/2 and the resulting classical Neumann conditions improves the understanding.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Mathematical Treatment:**  The paper provides a comprehensive and mathematically sound treatment of the coupling problem. Derivations are detailed, and justifications for assumptions are carefully provided.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The structure is well-organized, building from motivation to model development, analysis, and numerical results. The problem statement and key contributions are clearly defined.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The numerical experiments help to ground the theoretical findings in real-world applications.  They illustrate the qualitative behavior of the models and offer insight into parameter selection and model validation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The mathematical formalism can be challenging for readers without a strong background in functional analysis and nonlocal operators. The paper could benefit from more intuitive explanations of some of the key concepts.
    *   **Limited Scope of Numerical Experiments:**  The numerical section, while useful, could be expanded to include a broader range of problems and parameter settings to validate the model's performance more comprehensively. A quantitative comparison with analytical solutions (where possible) or benchmark problems would strengthen the results. A detailed analysis regarding the computation cost to use this approach would also benefit this paper.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the way researchers approach coupled local/nonlocal diffusion problems. The insights into transmission conditions and regularity can guide the development of more accurate and efficient numerical methods. The paper also provides a solid foundation for further research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper delivers a substantial contribution to the field, marked by rigorous analysis and practical relevance. While the complexity of the mathematical treatment and limited scope of numerical experiments reduce the score, it clearly moves the field forward. Therefore the score is considered as 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: math.NA
- **Score**: 7/10

### SGM: A Framework for Building Specification-Guided Moderation Filters
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19766v1)
- **Authors**: Masoomali Fatehkia, Enes Altinisik, Husrev Taha Sencar
- **Abstract**: Aligning large language models (LLMs) with deployment-specific requirements is critical but inherently imperfect. Despite extensive training, models remain susceptible to misalignment and adversarial inputs such as jailbreaks. Content moderation filters are commonly used as external safeguards, though they typically focus narrowly on safety. We introduce SGM (Specification-Guided Moderation), a flexible framework for training moderation filters grounded in user-defined specifications that go beyond standard safety concerns. SGM automates training data generation without relying on human-written examples, enabling scalable support for diverse, application-specific alignment goals. SGM-trained filters perform on par with state-of-the-art safety filters built on curated datasets, while supporting fine-grained and user-defined alignment control.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SGM (Specification-Guided Moderation), a novel framework for training content moderation filters for Large Language Models (LLMs). SGM aims to address the limitations of existing moderation filters, which often focus narrowly on safety and may not adequately capture domain-specific, value-driven, or institutionally-defined behavioral expectations. The key innovation of SGM lies in its automated training data generation process. It leverages meta-prompts to guide LLMs in generating diverse prompts and responses reflecting varying levels of compliance with user-defined specifications (policies). This process eliminates the need for extensive human-written examples, enabling scalable support for diverse alignment goals. The framework trains a regression-based alignment scoring model using the generated data, which then serves as the moderation filter. The paper demonstrates that SGM-trained filters perform competitively with state-of-the-art safety filters on public benchmarks, while also supporting custom alignment specifications and improving robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the automation of training data generation for moderation filters. While the use of LLMs for data augmentation is not entirely new, the specific application to content moderation and the use of meta-prompts to guide the generation process in alignment with complex user-defined specifications is a significant contribution. The approach allows for addressing alignment challenges that go beyond standard safety concerns and caters to a wide range of application-specific constraints.

*   **Significance:** The significance of SGM is that it provides a scalable and adaptable solution for aligning LLMs with diverse and evolving requirements. As LLMs are deployed in increasingly varied contexts, the ability to tailor moderation filters to specific domains, cultural norms, or organizational policies becomes critical. SGM offers a practical approach to achieve this customization without the need for manual data curation, lowering the barrier to entry for organizations with limited resources. The reported results showing competitive performance on public safety benchmarks further strengthens the significance of this contribution. Additionally, the observed cross-lingual transferability further increases the utility.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Automated training data generation for scalable alignment.
    *   Support for diverse and user-defined specifications beyond standard safety concerns.
    *   Competitive performance with state-of-the-art safety filters.
    *   Demonstrated cross-lingual transferability.
    *   Rigorous experiments and clear presentation of results.
    *   The open-source release of resources is a strong positive.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on LLMs for training data generation introduces a potential bias, although the meta-prompt design attempts to mitigate this. Further analysis of the types of biases introduced in the dataset generation phase would be beneficial.
    *   The current implementation is limited to single-turn conversations, restricting its applicability to more complex dialogue scenarios.
    *   While the paper demonstrated cross-lingual filtering from English into Arabic, a more thorough evaluation across a wider range of languages would strengthen the claim of cross-lingual generalization. The limitations of relying on LLMs for generating violations is a limitation.
    *   While the authors mention potential problems with abstract, conflicting and nuanced policies, further exploration of these edge cases could strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** SGM has the potential to influence the field of LLM alignment by providing a practical framework for building customizable moderation filters. It may encourage further research into automated data generation techniques and the development of more sophisticated meta-prompts for guiding LLM behavior. The open-source release of SGM's resources could accelerate the adoption of this approach and facilitate community-driven improvements.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM alignment by introducing SGM, a scalable and adaptable framework for training customizable moderation filters. The automated data generation process and competitive performance on public benchmarks demonstrate the practical value of this approach. The primary weaknesses are related to the reliance on LLMs for data generation and the limitation to single-turn conversations. However, the strengths of the paper outweigh these limitations, making it a valuable contribution with the potential to significantly impact the deployment of LLMs in diverse and application-specific settings. The clear presentation, rigorous experiments, and open-source release further enhance its value.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### TeViR: Text-to-Video Reward with Diffusion Models for Efficient Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19769v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhui Chen, Haoran Li, Zhennan Jiang, Haowei Wen, Dongbin Zhao
- **Abstract**: Developing scalable and generalizable reward engineering for reinforcement learning (RL) is crucial for creating general-purpose agents, especially in the challenging domain of robotic manipulation. While recent advances in reward engineering with Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have shown promise, their sparse reward nature significantly limits sample efficiency. This paper introduces TeViR, a novel method that leverages a pre-trained text-to-video diffusion model to generate dense rewards by comparing the predicted image sequence with current observations. Experimental results across 11 complex robotic tasks demonstrate that TeViR outperforms traditional methods leveraging sparse rewards and other state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods, achieving better sample efficiency and performance without ground truth environmental rewards. TeViR's ability to efficiently guide agents in complex environments highlights its potential to advance reinforcement learning applications in robotic manipulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TeViR: Text-to-Video Reward with Diffusion Models for Efficient Reinforcement Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TeViR, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) reward engineering method that leverages pre-trained text-to-video diffusion models.  TeViR generates dense rewards by comparing predicted future image sequences (based on the task description and current visual observation) with the current observed state.  This provides a more informative and continuous reward signal than sparse rewards or other reward engineering techniques.  The authors demonstrate that TeViR achieves superior sample efficiency and performance on a suite of robotic manipulation tasks in Meta-World, even without access to the ground truth environment reward. They further analyze the robustness of TeViR to noisy and imperfect video generation and ablate different components of the method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a text-to-video diffusion model to generate *dense* rewards for RL is relatively novel. While other works use pre-trained models for reward shaping, TeViR provides a trajectory-aware dense reward scheme rather than a sparse or short-horizon reward. The multi-view video generation to address occlusion issues is a good practical consideration, though less groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The ability to learn robotic manipulation tasks *without* relying on ground truth environment rewards is highly significant. It reduces the need for manual reward engineering, which is a major bottleneck in RL. The improved sample efficiency is also a valuable contribution, allowing for faster learning. Demonstrating robustness to imperfect video generation makes the method more practical for real-world scenarios where the models are not perfect.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of reward engineering in RL and motivates the need for a dense and generalizable reward function.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** TeViR is clearly described, and the algorithm is presented systematically.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental section is comprehensive, comparing TeViR against several relevant baselines and conducting ablation studies to analyze the contributions of individual components.  The evaluations cover a good range of tasks within Meta-World.  The robustness analysis to noisy video generation is a strong point.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The results consistently show that TeViR outperforms other methods in terms of sample efficiency and final performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Real-World Evaluation:** The experiments are conducted entirely in simulation using Meta-World. The performance of TeViR in real-world robotic tasks needs to be validated to assess its practical applicability. Sim2Real transfer is a known challenge and this is not addressed in the paper.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The text-to-video diffusion models are computationally expensive to train and run. The paper could provide more detail on the computational resources required for TeViR and potential ways to reduce the cost.
    *   **Dependency on Video Generation Quality:** Although the paper explores noisy video generation, the performance of TeViR is still tied to the quality of the diffusion model. If the diffusion model fails to generate realistic or relevant video sequences, the reward signal may be misleading.
    *   **Meta-World limitations:** While Meta-World is a good benchmark, it is arguably restrictive since it only allows tasks within a simulated robotics setting and the observations are not high-dimensional.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of reward engineering in RL, particularly for robotic manipulation. The idea of using pre-trained generative models to create dense rewards could be extended to other domains and combined with other techniques for improving exploration and generalization.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.

Here's the rationale: The core idea behind TeViR is novel and offers a significant advantage over existing reward engineering techniques by generating trajectory-aware *dense* rewards. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of TeViR in simulated robotic manipulation tasks, even without environmental rewards. The robustness analysis further strengthens the paper.

However, there are some weaknesses that prevent it from receiving a higher score. The lack of real-world experiments is a major limitation. Also, the method heavily relies on the power of pre-trained generative models. Additionally, a more in-depth analysis of failure cases would be valuable. Addressing these weaknesses would make the paper stronger and increase its impact. Therefore, the assigned score is 7, representing a strong contribution with potential for further refinement.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### What Really Matters in Many-Shot Attacks? An Empirical Study of Long-Context Vulnerabilities in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19773v1)
- **Authors**: Sangyeop Kim, Yohan Lee, Yongwoo Song, Kimin Lee
- **Abstract**: We investigate long-context vulnerabilities in Large Language Models (LLMs) through Many-Shot Jailbreaking (MSJ). Our experiments utilize context length of up to 128K tokens. Through comprehensive analysis with various many-shot attack settings with different instruction styles, shot density, topic, and format, we reveal that context length is the primary factor determining attack effectiveness. Critically, we find that successful attacks do not require carefully crafted harmful content. Even repetitive shots or random dummy text can circumvent model safety measures, suggesting fundamental limitations in long-context processing capabilities of LLMs. The safety behavior of well-aligned models becomes increasingly inconsistent with longer contexts. These findings highlight significant safety gaps in context expansion capabilities of LLMs, emphasizing the need for new safety mechanisms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates long-context vulnerabilities in Large Language Models (LLMs) using Many-Shot Jailbreaking (MSJ) attacks.  The authors conduct a comprehensive empirical study, varying factors such as context length (up to 128K tokens), instruction style, shot density, topic, and format.  The key finding is that context length is the *primary* factor determining attack success, surpassing the importance of carefully crafted harmful content. Surprisingly, repetitive or even random, meaningless text can circumvent model safety measures in long contexts, revealing fundamental limitations in how LLMs process extended inputs. The study shows that well-aligned models demonstrate increasingly inconsistent safety behavior as context length increases, emphasizing a significant safety gap in context expansion capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic and empirical investigation of long-context vulnerabilities in LLMs, challenging conventional assumptions about the need for specific harmful content in many-shot jailbreaking attacks.  The discovery that random or repetitive data can be as effective as, or even more effective than, harmful examples in long contexts is a novel and counter-intuitive finding.  While prior work has explored jailbreaking and long-context issues separately, this study bridges those gaps, and reveals significant interplay between these two factors.

* **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for the security and deployment of LLMs.  The study questions the efficacy of content-based defense mechanisms when dealing with long contexts and highlights the need for architectural-level safety measures. The inconsistent safety behaviour in well-aligned models with increasing context length poses a serious safety gap with the current strategy. This prompts the AI community to reconsider how safety is evaluated and reinforced as context length increases.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Empirical Study:**  The paper presents a thorough and well-designed set of experiments, covering a wide range of factors that could influence MSJ attack success.
    * **Counter-Intuitive Findings:** The discovery that harmfulness of the shots is not the main factor and the success of random data attacks challenge existing assumptions and opens up new potential attack vectors to investigate.
    * **Focus on Long-Context Issues:**  The work addresses a crucial and increasingly relevant area in LLM research, as context windows continue to grow.
    * **Clear Presentation:**  The paper is well-written and organized, with clear figures and tables summarizing the experimental results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Model Limitation:** The models investigated are open-source models that are smaller than the current models being developed.
    * **Evaluation metric:** The choice of ASR could be seen as a somewhat crude proxy for harmfulness. A more nuanced evaluation metric that takes into account the *severity* of harm could provide more insight. Also, though the research paper claims to provide an improvement to NLL, the experiment in detail is not convincing.
    * **Lack of Mechanistic Explanation:** While the paper identifies vulnerabilities, it provides limited mechanistic explanations for *why* these vulnerabilities exist.  Further research into the underlying mechanisms of long-context processing in LLMs is needed.

* **Potential Influence:** This research has the potential to influence the development of safer LLMs by:
    * Prompting the development of new defense mechanisms that are robust to long-context attacks, and are less reliant on content filtering.
    * Guiding future research into the limitations of current LLM architectures for processing long inputs.
    * Emphasizing the need for more comprehensive safety evaluations that consider long-context scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the paper's novel findings, its significant implications for LLM safety, the thoroughness of its empirical study, and the potential for its results to influence future research and development, a score of 7 is justified. The paper makes a substantial contribution to our understanding of LLM vulnerabilities, and prompts the AI community to rethink its approaches to ensuring the safety of LLMs in long-context scenarios. However, the limited mechanistic explanation and models tested prevents a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Done Is Better than Perfect: Unlocking Efficient Reasoning by Structured Multi-Turn Decomposition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19788v1)
- **Authors**: Zihao Zeng, Xuyao Huang, Boxiu Li, Hao Zhang, Zhijie Deng
- **Abstract**: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) are criticized for the excessively lengthy Chain-of-Thought (CoT) to derive the final answer, suffering from high first-token and overall latency. Typically, the CoT of LRMs mixes multiple thinking units; each unit attempts to produce a candidate answer to the original query. Hence, a natural idea to improve efficiency is to reduce the unit number. Yet, the fact that the thinking units in vanilla CoT cannot be explicitly managed renders doing so challenging. This paper introduces Multi-Turn Decomposition (MinD) to decode conventional CoT into a sequence of explicit, structured, and turn-wise interactions to bridge the gap. In MinD, the model provides a multi-turn response to the query, where each turn embraces a thinking unit and yields a corresponding answer. The subsequent turns can reflect, verify, revise, or explore alternative approaches to both the thinking and answer parts of earlier ones. This not only makes the answer delivered more swiftly, but also enables explicit controls over the iterative reasoning process (i.e., users may halt or continue at any turn). We follow a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) then reinforcement learning (RL) paradigm to realize MinD. We first rephrase the outputs of an LRM into multi-turn formats by prompting another LLM, and then tune the LRM with such data. Observing that the tuned model tends to consume even more tokens than the original one (probably due to that the multi-turn formats introduce additional answer tokens), we advocate leveraging RL algorithms like GRPO to prioritize correct outputs with fewer turns. Trained on the MATH dataset using R1-Distill models, MinD can achieve up to ~70% reduction in both output token usage and time to first token (TTFT), while maintaining competitive performance on reasoning benchmarks such as MATH-500, AIME24, AMC23, and GPQA-Diamond.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Multi-Turn Decomposition (MinD), a novel framework to enhance the efficiency of reasoning in Large Reasoning Models (LRMs).  MinD transforms the standard "think-then-answer" Chain-of-Thought (CoT) process into a structured sequence of multiple turns, where each turn involves a single reasoning unit followed by an answer.  This allows for explicit control over the reasoning process and enables the model to provide candidate answers earlier in the reasoning chain. The authors implement MinD using a supervised fine-tuning (SFT) followed by reinforcement learning (RL) approach with GRPO, demonstrating significant reductions in token usage and latency while maintaining competitive performance across various reasoning benchmarks. The key idea is to address the redundancy often seen in CoT reasoning by decomposing the process into smaller, manageable units and penalizing unnecessary turns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of decomposing CoT into multi-turn reasoning is conceptually novel. While previous works have focused on shortening CoT or using latent spaces, MinD directly addresses the granularity of reasoning by making individual thinking units explicit and controllable. The explicit multi-turn structure and use of GRPO to reduce redundancy are also notable contributions. However, the reliance on SFT and GRPO, which are existing techniques, somewhat diminishes the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates practical improvements in reasoning efficiency. Reducing token usage and latency is crucial for deploying LRMs in real-world applications. The results show substantial reductions in token usage and TTFT while maintaining accuracy across a range of benchmarks, including out-of-distribution tasks. This suggests that MinD can potentially make LRMs more practical and cost-effective. The framework’s generalizability is also appealing.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the inefficiency of standard CoT reasoning due to redundancy.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** The MinD framework is well-defined and explained, with clear steps for implementation.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of MinD in reducing token usage and latency while maintaining accuracy. The inclusion of out-of-distribution benchmarks enhances the credibility of the approach.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components of the framework, particularly the role of GRPO.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Existing Techniques:** MinD relies heavily on SFT and GRPO, which are not new. The primary contribution lies in the application and integration of these techniques within the multi-turn reasoning framework.
    *   **GPT-4 Dependence:** The decomposition of CoT traces depends on GPT-4's ability to delineate the sub-steps. If GPT-4 were to underperform, or if its underlying algorithms change drastically, the results may become difficult to reproduce.
    *   **Limited Model and Task Scope:** The experiments are primarily focused on mathematical reasoning with the DeepSeek-R1-Distill models (1.5B and 7B). Further evaluation with larger models and other reasoning tasks would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Parameter Tuning:** A limited amount of parameter tuning may limit the quality of results.
    *   **Over-Reliance on GPT-4:** While the paper discusses decomposing CoT traces using GPT-4, there is no exploration of other methods or algorithms.

*   **Potential Influence:** The MinD framework has the potential to influence future research on efficient reasoning in LRMs. It provides a new perspective on how to structure and control the reasoning process. The explicit control over reasoning units may also enable new applications, such as interactive reasoning and adaptive reasoning based on problem difficulty or user preference.

**Justification of Score:**

While MinD builds upon existing techniques like SFT and GRPO, it offers a novel approach to structuring the reasoning process in LRMs, leading to significant efficiency gains. The multi-turn decomposition and explicit control over reasoning units have the potential to influence future research on efficient reasoning. However, the reliance on existing techniques and the limited scope of experiments somewhat limit its impact.

Therefore, a score of 7 is assigned.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Missing Point in Vision Transformers for Universal Image Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19795v1)
- **Authors**: Sajjad Shahabodini, Mobina Mansoori, Farnoush Bayatmakou, Jamshid Abouei, Konstantinos N. Plataniotis, Arash Mohammadi
- **Abstract**: Image segmentation remains a challenging task in computer vision, demanding robust mask generation and precise classification. Recent mask-based approaches yield high-quality masks by capturing global context. However, accurately classifying these masks, especially in the presence of ambiguous boundaries and imbalanced class distributions, remains an open challenge. In this work, we introduce ViT-P, a novel two-stage segmentation framework that decouples mask generation from classification. The first stage employs a proposal generator to produce class-agnostic mask proposals, while the second stage utilizes a point-based classification model built on the Vision Transformer (ViT) to refine predictions by focusing on mask central points. ViT-P serves as a pre-training-free adapter, allowing the integration of various pre-trained vision transformers without modifying their architecture, ensuring adaptability to dense prediction tasks. Furthermore, we demonstrate that coarse and bounding box annotations can effectively enhance classification without requiring additional training on fine annotation datasets, reducing annotation costs while maintaining strong performance. Extensive experiments across COCO, ADE20K, and Cityscapes datasets validate the effectiveness of ViT-P, achieving state-of-the-art results with 54.0 PQ on ADE20K panoptic segmentation, 87.4 mIoU on Cityscapes semantic segmentation, and 63.6 mIoU on ADE20K semantic segmentation. The code and pretrained models are available at: https://github.com/sajjad-sh33/ViT-P}{https://github.com/sajjad-sh33/ViT-P.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "The Missing Point in Vision Transformers for Universal Image Segmentation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of accurate mask classification in mask-based image segmentation. While recent approaches generate high-quality masks, classifying these masks, especially with ambiguous boundaries and imbalanced classes, remains difficult. The authors introduce ViT-P, a two-stage segmentation framework that decouples mask generation from classification. The first stage produces class-agnostic mask proposals, and the second stage uses a point-based classification model built on Vision Transformers (ViT) to refine predictions by focusing on mask central points. ViT-P acts as a pre-training-free adapter, integrating various pre-trained vision transformers.  The paper also explores optimized annotation strategies, using coarse and bounding box annotations to supplement fine annotations, reducing annotation costs. Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art or competitive performance on COCO, ADE20K, and Cityscapes datasets across panoptic, instance, and semantic segmentation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of decoupling mask generation and classification is not entirely new, but ViT-P's specific implementation with point-based classification and its pre-training-free adapter approach are novel.  The exploration of using coarse and bounding box annotations to improve classification accuracy is also a valuable contribution. The exploitation of ViT for classification points instead of entire masks is a good twist to the traditional approach.

* **Significance:** The paper tackles a real bottleneck in mask-based segmentation – mask classification accuracy. By improving this aspect, ViT-P contributes to more accurate and efficient segmentation. The pre-training-free adapter design offers flexibility in leveraging various pre-trained vision transformers. The use of coarser annotations offers a practical advantage by reducing annotation costs, which is crucial for scaling up segmentation models. This would be significant if the performance increase from coarser annotations was more substantial than from the more detailed annotation schemes. The work is demonstrated on widely-used datasets.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of mask classification accuracy.
    * **Novel Approach:** The point-based classification and the decoupling of mask generation from classification are innovative.
    * **Pre-training-free Adapter:** The flexibility of integrating pre-trained ViTs without modification is a key strength.
    * **Practical Optimization:** The annotation strategy is designed to reduce costs.
    * **Strong Results:** State-of-the-art or competitive results are demonstrated on standard benchmarks.
    * **Ablation Studies:** Inclusion of ablation studies helped better show the benefit of their approach.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty:** The general idea of decoupling is present in the literature.
    * **Dependency on Proposal Generator:**  The performance is still dependent on the quality of the mask proposals.  If the proposal generator fails to produce accurate masks, ViT-P will not be able to classify them correctly.
    * **Marginal Gains on Some Tasks:** Instance and panoptic segmentation gains are only marginal for some datasets.
    * **Limited Evaluation of Annotation Strategy:** The impact of the coarse annotation strategy, while mentioned, could have been better explored, with more detailed experiments demonstrating the trade-off between annotation cost and accuracy gains.
    * **Reliance on ensembling:** The improved performance is largely dependent on combining the outputs of their framework with the proposal mechanism.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in image segmentation by promoting decoupled architectures and point-based classification methods. It could also encourage the development of more cost-effective annotation strategies. Widespread implementation of the core features in this paper by other researchers depends upon them being able to reproduce some of the success with different datasets and parameters.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of image segmentation, addressing a relevant problem and offering a novel solution. The pre-training-free adapter design and the optimized annotation strategy are valuable additions. While the improvements in instance and panoptic segmentation are marginal, the overall results are promising. The limited novelty of the decoupling idea prevents a higher score. However, the overall novelty, significance and experimental evaluation warrants a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MOLE: Metadata Extraction and Validation in Scientific Papers Using LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19800v1)
- **Authors**: Zaid Alyafeai, Maged S. Al-Shaibani, Bernard Ghanem
- **Abstract**: Metadata extraction is essential for cataloging and preserving datasets, enabling effective research discovery and reproducibility, especially given the current exponential growth in scientific research. While Masader (Alyafeai et al.,2021) laid the groundwork for extracting a wide range of metadata attributes from Arabic NLP datasets' scholarly articles, it relies heavily on manual annotation. In this paper, we present MOLE, a framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically extract metadata attributes from scientific papers covering datasets of languages other than Arabic. Our schema-driven methodology processes entire documents across multiple input formats and incorporates robust validation mechanisms for consistent output. Additionally, we introduce a new benchmark to evaluate the research progress on this task. Through systematic analysis of context length, few-shot learning, and web browsing integration, we demonstrate that modern LLMs show promising results in automating this task, highlighting the need for further future work improvements to ensure consistent and reliable performance. We release the code: https://github.com/IVUL-KAUST/MOLE and dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/IVUL-KAUST/MOLE for the research community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MOLE paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MOLE, a framework leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate metadata extraction from scientific papers about datasets, focusing on languages other than English. The framework utilizes a schema-driven approach, processing entire documents from various input formats and incorporates validation mechanisms for consistent output. They also introduce a new benchmark with datasets in multiple languages to evaluate performance.  The authors explore the effects of context length, few-shot learning, and web browsing integration, demonstrating the potential of LLMs for this task. The code and dataset are released to facilitate further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Automation of metadata extraction:** The framework aims to automate a time-consuming and labor-intensive task, which is increasingly crucial due to the exponential growth of scientific data.
    *   **Schema-driven Approach:**  Using a schema to guide the LLM's extraction promotes structured and consistent output, which is essential for building usable metadata repositories.
    *   **Comprehensive Metadata:** The extraction of 30+ distinct attributes per paper is a significant improvement over existing methods, which typically focus on a much smaller subset.
    *   **Multilingual Benchmark:** Addressing languages beyond English fills a gap and makes the benchmark more representative of the global research landscape. The inclusion of languages like Arabic, Russian, Japanese, and French is commendable.
    *   **LLM Analysis:** The systematic analysis of multiple LLMs (proprietary and open-source) and investigation into parameters like context length and few-shot learning provides valuable insights. The inclusion of web browsing integration is innovative, allowing the models to retrieve information that may not be present in the paper itself.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The release of code and dataset is a major strength, enabling other researchers to build upon and improve the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The framework's performance is intrinsically tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLMs. Inconsistencies and errors in LLM outputs could propagate through the system, impacting the overall reliability of metadata extraction. The paper does acknowledge this and highlights the need for further work to ensure consistent performance.
    *   **Dependency on LaTeX:** The framework is very dependetnt on LaTeX which is not always available and can impact performance if PDF is used instead
    *   **Limited Scalability discussion:** While the paper shows results with different context lengths, there isn't a detailed analysis of the cost-performance trade-off for very large-scale applications, which is a real-world challenge. How to optimize the framework for processing thousands of papers efficiently needs more elaboration.
    *   **Depth of Error Analysis:** While the paper presents aggregate results, a deeper dive into the types of errors made (e.g., incorrect attribute identification, inability to handle specific document structures) would be more informative. A more detailed error analysis, linking specific error types to the architectural choices or prompt strategies, would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Generalizability Beyond NLP:** The work is primarily focused on NLP datasets. The question of how MOLE would adapt to datasets in other scientific domains (e.g., biomedicine, materials science) requires further consideration.  Metadata schemas are highly domain-specific; the authors need to address the effort involved in adapting MOLE to new domains.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   MOLE represents a significant step towards automating comprehensive metadata extraction from scientific papers. The focus on multiple languages, the comprehensive metadata schema, and the incorporation of modern LLMs are noteworthy.
    *   The benchmark dataset is valuable for the community and can drive further research in this area.
    *   The analysis of LLM performance, particularly regarding context length and few-shot learning, adds to our understanding of how to effectively use these models for information extraction tasks.
    *   While the work is promising, it builds upon existing metadata extraction techniques and recent advancements in LLMs. The contribution is more in the integration and application of these technologies to a specific problem, rather than a completely novel theoretical breakthrough.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a useful framework with a focus on automation in extracting metadata and builds a valuable benchmark for the scientific community. However, there are opportunities to further refine the framework to address limitations mentioned such as reliance on LLMs, dependency on LaTeX source availability, limited scalability, and the depth of error analysis and generalizability.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Compliance-to-Code: Enhancing Financial Compliance Checking via Code Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19804v1)
- **Authors**: Siyuan Li, Jian Chen, Rui Yao, Xuming Hu, Peilin Zhou, Weihua Qiu, Simin Zhang, Chucheng Dong, Zhiyao Li, Qipeng Xie, Zixuan Yuan
- **Abstract**: Nowadays, regulatory compliance has become a cornerstone of corporate governance, ensuring adherence to systematic legal frameworks. At its core, financial regulations often comprise highly intricate provisions, layered logical structures, and numerous exceptions, which inevitably result in labor-intensive or comprehension challenges. To mitigate this, recent Regulatory Technology (RegTech) and Large Language Models (LLMs) have gained significant attention in automating the conversion of regulatory text into executable compliance logic. However, their performance remains suboptimal particularly when applied to Chinese-language financial regulations, due to three key limitations: (1) incomplete domain-specific knowledge representation, (2) insufficient hierarchical reasoning capabilities, and (3) failure to maintain temporal and logical coherence. One promising solution is to develop a domain specific and code-oriented datasets for model training. Existing datasets such as LexGLUE, LegalBench, and CODE-ACCORD are often English-focused, domain-mismatched, or lack fine-grained granularity for compliance code generation. To fill these gaps, we present Compliance-to-Code, the first large-scale Chinese dataset dedicated to financial regulatory compliance. Covering 1,159 annotated clauses from 361 regulations across ten categories, each clause is modularly structured with four logical elements-subject, condition, constraint, and contextual information-along with regulation relations. We provide deterministic Python code mappings, detailed code reasoning, and code explanations to facilitate automated auditing. To demonstrate utility, we present FinCheck: a pipeline for regulation structuring, code generation, and report generation.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces Compliance-to-Code, a large-scale Chinese dataset specifically designed for financial regulatory compliance. The dataset consists of 1,159 annotated clauses from 361 regulations across ten categories. Each clause is structured with four logical elements (subject, condition, constraint, and contextual information) and regulation relations, coupled with deterministic Python code mappings, detailed code reasoning, and explanations for automated auditing. The authors also present FinCheck, a pipeline for regulation structuring, code generation, and report generation, demonstrating the dataset's utility. Experimental results show that fine-tuning Qwen3-8B with Compliance-to-Code significantly improves both structural parsing and compliance code generation.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper addresses a significant gap in the field of RegTech by providing a large-scale, structured, Chinese dataset tailored for financial regulatory compliance, a domain where existing resources are limited, especially in non-English languages.  The novelty lies in the combination of several factors:
